Mercedes Delays U.S. GLC Production, Leaving the Model Vulnerable to Tariffs

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

The brand’s bread-and-butter crossover won’t be made in the United States until the next generation launches.

Mercedes-Benz is delaying the start of its U.S. production of the GLC-class crossover, according to Automotive News. The automaker had originally planned for production of the GLC to begin in late 2027, but the paper reports that it has now been delayed to 2030. When the U.S. production does begin, it will take place at the automaker’s Vance, Alabama, plant.

According to the AN report, Mercedes is delaying the move in production to wait for the arrival of the next-generation GLC-class. “Investing in the plant for about half a life cycle and then having to retool for the next-generation GLC doesn’t make a lot of sense financially,” Sam Fiorani, the vice president of AutoForecast, told the outlet. The Alabama plant, in its current configuration, is home to the GLS-class and GLE-class SUVs, along with their electric counterparts.

See all results for new 2027 Mercedes-Benz GLC-Class for sale near 98837

The updated timeline lines up with the expected arrival of the next-generation GLC-class; the current model received its most recent refresh in 2023. Unfortunately, the delay leaves the GLC-class, which is one of the most important models for the brand in the United States, exposed to increased costs related to import tariffs. Mercedes has previously committed to spending some $4 billion at its Alabama plant to localize the production of its high-volume vehicles, which are currently subject to 15 percent import tariffs.

On the flip side, some reports suggest that the Trump administration is considering lowering its tariff on European cars to 10 percent in an attempt to sway Audi into building a planned $4 billion Georgia plant. Car and Driver reached out to Mercedes-Benz for confirmation of the AN report but has not heard back at the time of this article’s publication. We will update this story when we learn more.

Jack Fitzgerald’s love for cars stems from his as yet unshakable addiction to Formula 1. After a brief stint as a detailer for a local dealership group in college, he knew he needed a more permanent way to drive all the new cars he couldn’t afford and decided to pursue a career in auto writing. By hounding his college professors at the University of Wisconsin-Milwaukee, he was able to travel Wisconsin seeking out stories in the auto world before landing his dream job at Car and Driver. His new goal is to delay the inevitable demise of his 2010 Volkswagen Golf.

Pro-crypto candidates sweep 241 seats as Congress shifts power

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

The group's unofficial tally names CLARITY backers among the winners, but its ratings do not establish votes for passage.

Crypto advocacy group Stand With Crypto (SWC) counted 222 House winners and 19 Senate winners it rates “pro-crypto” in its Nov. 4 election scorecard, identifying allies for the industry.

The group's results page also listed 106 House winners and eight Senate winners it rated “anti-crypto.” Its tally was marked updated at 9 a.m. ET and showed 384 of 435 House races and 33 of 35 Senate races called.

The group credits Decision Desk HQ for results and says they remain unofficial until certified. The pro-crypto and anti-crypto totals are also smaller than the number of called races displayed for each chamber.

SWC counts winning allies. CLARITY still needs agreement.

“America still loves crypto, but hates the physical manifestation of AI.”

Reddit Begins Testing Ads In Its Own Search Results via @sejournal, @MattGSouthern

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

This webinar uses Conductor's research to help you prioritize marketing data and AI visibility measurement fixes.

This rundown shows how to test briefs, audits, or rank reporting with a four-layer system built on your own data.

This webinar uses Conductor's research to help you prioritize marketing data and AI visibility measurement fixes.

This rundown shows how to test briefs, audits, or rank reporting with a four-layer system built on your own data.

Ask the SEJ experts you already read what AI, agents, and algorithm changes mean for your traffic. Private, human to human. $97/mo.

9 AI Workflows For Better Content. Nov 18, 2 PM ET.

Ads are being tested in the community platform's own search results with a select group of advertisers, matched partly by relevance to what people search.

Reddit is testing ads in its own search results pages with a select group of eligible advertisers.

The company announced the test in a short newsroom post on Tuesday and said it will evaluate how users respond so it can refine the experience over time. The post doesn’t say how advertisers were chosen or whether others can ask to join.

“These placements leverage existing ad targeting in search results pages on Reddit, which have high purchase and search intent.”

“Ads in search may appear based on a range of factors, including contextual relevance to a user’s search query.”

The post does not mention what else would factor into which ads appear. In the example, a person searching for “espresso station” might see ads for coffee beans, mugs, or espresso machines, and “home office setup” may lead to advertisements for desks, office chairs, or monitors.

Example screens in the post show the mobile version of the Reddit application after searching for “home office setup”. Each screen shows a card labeled “Ad” containing a picture of a product (with a website address) and a “Shop Now” button. One example shows a card sitting under a blurb citing subreddits as its sources. Another example shows a card placed between two posts.

Brooke Osmundson reported that in October 2024, Reddit extended the keyword targeting it uses in its conversation placements to the Feed of the platform.

In its first quarter shareholder letter, Reddit stated that the product placements allow users searching on the site to browse listings and shop right from within those search results. In a phone call with investors after their Q1 earnings were released (April 30), Chief Operating Officer Jennifer Wong stated that Reddit didn’t “even have ads in certain high growing surfaces like search, for example.” On this same call, CEO Steve Huffman reported that “Search WAUqs are up 30% year-over-year.” Search WAUqs is Reddit’s shorthand for weekly active uniques who search.

During a Q2 investor call, Wong referred to search as “a space that we’re eyeing” for advertising and mentioned that Reddit has the infrastructure “already queued up,” according to Roger Montti. Wong also referenced Shopping Listing Ads, a format that groups several retailers’ products in one module, as examples of the type of advertisements she believes could be used on a search page.

Beyond eligibility, Reddit’s post is also silent regarding the other key elements of how the test works, such as markets, ad format types, ad pricing models, and whether it covers more than the app used in its examples.

Reddit is pitching its search pages to advertisers on the strength of what people type there. For now, Reddit’s search ads are limited to the advertisers it selected for the test. You can ask your Reddit rep whether your account is part of the test and how search impressions would show up in reporting.

Advertisers outside the test can watch for search to appear among Reddit’s ad placements. The post gives no end date for the test and no timeline for adding advertisers.

Follow on YouTube. Matt G. Southern is the Senior News Writer at Search Engine Journal, where he’s covered Google, SEO, ...

How To Initiate Sex Again After A Long Drought, Because We've All Been There

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

It’s all about easing in (and examining what got you here.)

Work, kids, family obligations, running your household, a hobby if you’re lucky enough to have time for one — all of these things take time and energy. It’s enough to have you laid out on the couch, carcass style, at the end of a regular day. When something actually goes wrong, like the kids get sick or a work project keeps you at your desk late, it’s even harder to imagine getting it up at the end of the day. No matter how much you love your spouse, sex droughts come for everyone at some point (ahem, many points) throughout a relationship. If it’s been a while and you want to get back in the game, here’s how to reinitiate.

Even when you’ve been married for years, once you and your partner get in a routine of not having sex, it can feel awkward or strange to try and start it up again. It almost feels like you have to acknowledge the drought, and therefore the bad things that led up to it, or perhaps the parts you’ve both played in letting it drag on. It’s just a lot, but try not to blame yourself.

“Sex droughts are far more common than pop culture would have us believe. They're certainly nothing to be ashamed of, nor do they represent a ‘failure,’” says Angie Rowntree, ABS, board-certified sexologist and founder and filmmaker behind the award-winning ethical adult film site Sssh.com. “Life happens. Bodies get sick or injured, or may have different needs. Children are born and demand your time and care. Older parents may need care. Jobs might change. Any number of stresses and interruptions in your life can upend what was your regularly scheduled Saturday date night or sap you of the carefree mojo you’d usually bring to the bedroom. None of that is a shortcoming, or is anyone’s fault.”

Every couple is different in terms of how much sex is enough for them to feel happy with it, so remember that you don’t have to meet some made-up quota of intimate evenings per week. If you are ready to get back into the groove, though, here’s how to start.

First, initiating sex after a long break comes with natural stress and pressure. If it doesn’t land with your spouse, don’t let it get you down.

“Sometimes we need a little warm-up before we get back in the saddle, so to speak. Start small. Don’t hint with memes, but act. Physical touch and flirtation are things that ignite foreplay in the mind. Kiss for a little longer than usual before you leave. Send a suggestive text during the day. Get into bed and cuddle, but without immediately reaching below the belt. You’re essentially reminding each other that you still desire each other,” Rowntree says.

It’s also totally OK to address the elephant in the room, Rowntree says. Tell your partner, “I miss being close to you. Can we make some time tonight?” This way you’re giving them an invitation and creating a little anticipation all at once.

If you go for it and your partner turns you down, don’t turn a situational no into a personal one, says Rowntree.

“When they say, ‘Honey, I’m tired,’ it becomes, ‘You don’t turn me on anymore.’ The longer you’ve been together, the more history there can be attached to that moment. If one person is usually initiating sex, or usually declining it, those long-term behavior patterns can make a huge impact.”

If there’s longstanding angst in your relationship around who initiates and how often, that in and of itself can lead to more sex droughts. It’s something to have a “frank discussion” about, Rowntree advises. During that conversation, you want to figure out how to separate “I don’t want sex right now” from “I don’t want you.”

“You want to frame your communication in a way that maintains your partner’s desirability and your interest. For instance: ‘I’m exhausted tonight, but can we cuddle?’ or, ‘Why don’t I wake you up and we can take a shower together tomorrow morning before I have to go?’ The point is, you’re declining the timing, not rejecting your partner, or giving them any ambiguity about your attraction to them.”

If issues around initiating are keeping you two on a holding pattern sex-wise, again, talk about it. Find a time when you have privacy and are fully clothed so there’s no immediate pressure, and hash out the details of each other’s wants, needs, and preferences around initiating. Rowntree says you should ask each other:

“Sometimes it really is the little things that turn a ‘meh, maybe’ into a spark that goes all the way. Also, please stop feeling like you need elaborate Instagram-worthy seduction schemes. There’s no reason to overcomplicate basic human desire. Put on music and pour a drink together after work. Send a flirty text before you get home. Give them a lingering kiss in the kitchen that morning. Or just say, ‘Why don’t we go to bed early tonight? I’ve missed you.’”

The next time you use Clear to get through airport security, it might be faster than you've come to expect.

Over the course of this year, Clear has been expanding its new “e-gates” to dozens of airports across the country.

It's a more streamlined setup that allows passengers to fly through without stopping at one of its famous blue kiosks.

As of this week, Clear had deployed its new automated gates to 53 airports across the country.

By the end of the year, the tech is expected to be in place at nearly all the airports where the company offers its flagship Clear Plus service.

That's certainly good news for travelers looking to speed through Transportation Security Administration checkpoints — especially as the busy holiday travel rush looms.

In the past, travelers hoping to use Clear at TSA would walk up to the checkpoint, then peel off to get their identity verified with a quick photo. Then, a Clear staffer would escort them to the front of the TSA line.

While it's obviously a shortcut, the service hasn't been without pain points.

Sometimes, flyers would arrive to find no open kiosks, or have to wait for Clear staffers to be free to assist them.

The new e-gate setup is intended to improve the flow of passengers.

I've used the Clear e-gates at a handful of airports this year, and am typically able to walk through pretty seamlessly — often pausing for just a few seconds to get my photo taken before the gates open.

The company touts identity verification in five seconds or less under the new design (but do let us know in the comments what your experience was like).

As of this week, Clear had installed e-gates at nearly 80% of the airports it serves — and noted the "vast majority" of its network would have the e-gates by the end of this year.

At those hubs, the company said 96% passengers were getting through in five minutes or less.

These improvements should make the service an even better shortcut at more airports.

Clear is a paid service that lets passengers effectively skip the TSA line and jump right to carry-on baggage screening. It’s typically maximized in tandem with TSA PreCheck access.

Clear’s flagship airport security service (known as Clear Plus) now costs $219 for an annual membership.

But some travel cards will offset the cost. For instance, I get a statement credit of up to $219 each year for my Clear+ membership fee by carrying the American Express Platinum Card®, which has an annual fee of $895 (see rates and fees). Enrollment required; subject to auto-renewal.

Plus, three major U.S. airlines partner with Clear to offer special pricing: Delta Air Lines, United Airlines and Alaska Airlines.

TPG Tip: If you want to try Clear and sign up using TPG's special offer, you can save $40 on the first year's membership.

Even as Clear expands its e-gates at TSA checkpoints, the program has also gotten some friendly competition from the TSA's newest security shortcut called TSA PreCheck Touchless ID.

That service, which allows members to pass through security without pulling out their ID or boarding pass, is now available at dozens of airports across the country. TSA Touchless has no extra cost for PreCheck (and Global Entry) members, though you have to opt in via your airline profile.

Report: Arsenal star's agents seek bombshell transfer to Liverpool

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

We could well see one Arsenal star move to Liverpool. His agents have sought a move and made contact with the Reds.

Arsenal and Liverpool have found themselves as rivals over the last few years, even if they haven’t actually battled in a serious title race yet. The Gunners ultimately didn’t come close to stopping the Reds in 2025, while Liverpool’s efforts in 2026 were even worse.

Yet, there’s a sense that this is the title fight for the next few years. If Liverpool can sort themselves out anyway, and we wait on the outcome of Manchester City’s guilty verdict.

As a result, no one should really expect much transfer activity between the two. Even if you don’t rate one of your players, sending them to a rival who does rate them is a recipe for disaster. It usually just means you’re undervaluing your player, in fact.

But that’s not to say it’s impossible. One Arsenal star, it’s reported, has been in contact with Liverpool over a possible move.

Martin Zubimendi to Liverpool?Liverpool tried to sign Martin Zubimendi in 2024 from Real Sociedad. It was Arne Slot's first summer and the Reds felt he was the ideal player for the new head coach. Zubimendi, however, pulled out of the deal at the last minute and remained with La Real for another year. He'd then move to Arsenal in 2025, having decided to make a big move after all. That ended successfully for Zubimendi to an extent. He was a key part of their Premier League title success but Arsenal have already replaced him. The Spaniard now barely plays and the Gunners are willing to sell.

Liverpool tried to sign Martin Zubimendi in 2024 from Real Sociedad. It was Arne Slot's first summer and the Reds felt he was the ideal player for the new head coach.

Zubimendi, however, pulled out of the deal at the last minute and remained with La Real for another year. He'd then move to Arsenal in 2025, having decided to make a big move after all.

That ended successfully for Zubimendi to an extent. He was a key part of their Premier League title success but Arsenal have already replaced him. The Spaniard now barely plays and the Gunners are willing to sell.

We've already called that a mistake on their part and one that Liverpool should take advantage of. It appears the opportunity is there, too.

TeamTalk claim that Zubimendi's representatives have contacted Liverpool about a possible move. It's not really known if the Reds are willing to make an offer at this stage, nor can we say what the price tag would be, but there is a chance of it happening.

Zubimendi almost certainly moves in 2027. Liverpool almost certainly sign a midfielder, too. Whether that's a 27-year-old, though, is another matter - such a signing would go against everything they've been doing lately.

Still, we believe he's the ideal fit for this team and exactly what's needed in the middle. Honestly, if Zubimendi and Arsenal are willing, Liverpool would be silly not to bid.

The MCU’s Multiverse Saga is nearly over, but there are plenty more Marvel movies and shows on the horizon.

When you purchase through links on our site, we may earn an affiliate commission. Here’s how it works.

Marvel fatigue? Think again, especially after Spider-Man: Brand New Day made over $2.5 billion worldwide and Avengers: Doomsday is looking hot, hot, hot!

But even after the uneven Multiverse Saga ends (hopefully on a high note), Marvel Studios won’t be stopping. So, we've put together a list of all the upcoming MCU movies and shows confirmed so far.

While the MCU isn't stopping, Marvel has slowed its roll, with the powers that be returning to the “quality over quantity” approach that defined Marvel Studios’ Infinity Saga output. This means no more than three movies per year and, perhaps more importantly, severely cutting down on TV productions, with a focus on multi-season shows rather than limited series that should’ve been a movie.

It’s a time of change for both Disney and Marvel, but it seems everyone agrees the big screen reigns supreme, so expect a thinner streaming output and more relevant movies starring characters you actually know.

Earth’s Mightiest Heroes (from different Earths, actually) are assembling again in December 2026.

Avengers: Doomsday's cast looks ridiculously stacked, and it’ll finally realize the dream of bringing together the Avengers, the Fantastic Four, and the X-Men (Fox’s original ones, that is) on the big screen. It’s a lot to absorb, and we don’t really know what's going on, but the trailers released so far are promising.

Anthony & Joe Russo (Captain America: The Winter Soldier & Civil War, Avengers: Infinity War & Endgame) are returning to direct, so that’s a good reason to be hyped even if the duo’s post-Marvel career hasn’t been the most exciting. Stephen McFeely (one of their two go-to scribes) is also back, and Michael Waldron (Loki) is helping out as well. It’s an all-star team for the most ambitious MCU project to date, at least until it all culminates in...

Release window: March 2027 | Showrunner: Dario Scardapane | Cast: Charlie Cox, Mike Colter, Finn Jones, Krysten Ritter

Daredevil: Born Again’s debut season was far from perfect, but most viewers agreed that Season 2 was a marked improvement.

Hopefully, that energy will continue in the third and final outing, as the Defenders reunite, with Finn Jones, Krysten Ritter, and Mike Colter all returning from their Netflix shows. Another shocker is the (unofficially) confirmed return of Elodie Yung’s Elektra.

We suspect some of these characters will be transferred to movies post-Secret Wars. In any case, Born Again’s final season should be packed, especially if Marvel Television is sticking to eight or nine episodes.

Release date: December 17, 2027 | Director: Anthony & Joe Russo | Cast: Chris Hemsworth, Chris Evans, Pedro Pascal, Robert Downey Jr.

Practically nothing about the follow-up to Avengers: Doomsday has been revealed yet (though we assume the MCU’s main players will be part of the ensemble), but we know it fully starts shooting in late 2026 and is eyeing a Christmas 2027 arrival. The plan is to leave a year-long wait between the two event movies, leaving fans hanging like back in the MCU’s glory days.

The title comes from Jonathan Hickman’s Secret Wars (2015), which is probably the main source of inspiration for the upcoming big-screen event. Incursions (realities bleeding into each other) have already been set up during the Multiverse Saga, and we know Doomsday directly deals with the multiverse’s breaking point, so it’s easy to see where the larger picture is going.

No spoilers here, but prepare for a very different kind of Avengers flick if Battleworld is indeed the main setting.

Release date: May 5, 2028 | Director: Jake Schreier | Cast: Kit Connor, Sadie Sink, Adam Driver, Christopher Abbott

Whatever happens in Secret Wars (we’ve heard Marvel Studios intends to soft-reboot), Phase Seven of the MCU will kick off with a long-awaited, fresh take on the X-Men — now part of the larger Marvel universe housing all characters. Thunderbolts* director Jake Schreier will be directing, with the same writing team from that movie joining him for another Marvel adventure.

Spider-Man: Brand New Day already did some heavy lifting when it came to setting up one of its leads (Sadie Sink’s Jean Grey), but it’s unclear how the whole group will be assembled.

We know for sure Cyclops (Kit Connor), Rogue (Inde Navarrette), Emma Frost (Samara Weaving), Storm (Maya Boyd), Angel (Asa Germann), Charles Xavier (Christopher Abbott), and Mr. Sinister (Adam Driver) are confirmed to appear, but the cast should grow in the coming months.

The duo worked together on Star Wars: Starfighter, and it seems it went so well that Disney and Marvel are happy with them reviving the Spirit of Vengeance as the MCU expands its dark borders.

It’s too early to even know which Ghost Rider that Gosling will be playing (Johnny Blaze or Danny Ketch) or where the plot is going, but considering Levy’s past movies, we’re expecting an adventurous tone that blends well with Ghost Rider’s very striking, hellish visuals and darker themes.

Of course, this project is an invitation to add more magical beings to the MCU and explore the Strange-adjacent side of it. Maybe it’s our first step towards a Midnight Sons event movie? We can only hope.

Release date: December 15, 2028 | Director: Ryan Coogler | Cast: David Jonsson, Denzel Washington, Letitia Wright, Winston Duke

Ryan Coogler has taken a break from Marvel to make the excellent Sinners and help create a new iteration of The X-Files, but he’s confirmed to be coming back to wrap up his epic Black Panther trilogy. A while back, he confirmed he’d convinced Denzel Washington to join the MCU, and it sounds like those plans haven’t changed.

We also have a new T’Challa: David Jonsson (Alien: Romulus), who’s meant to play the son of the original T’Challa (Chadwick Boseman). If the years don’t make sense considering he was a kid in Wakanda Forever, wait until Secret Wars’ “soft reboot” for the answers.

What we know for sure is that series veterans Letitia Wright and Winston Duke are expected to stick around, but no plot details are available yet.

Revealed at NYCC 2026, Zodiac is set to be Marvel Television’s next ongoing series after Daredevil: Born Again is over. Little is known about the premise at this point, though TV boss Brad Winderbaum has confirmed it’s a “globetrotting, international espionage show about secrets and lies and betrayals” with a packed cast of characters.

Word is the show is, more specifically, a British spy thriller with three leads, two male and one female. It’s expected to shoot in London during spring 2027, and chances are we’ll know much more about it well before that.

Of course, longtime Marvel TV fans are hoping for an Agents of SHIELD revival of sorts (its canonicity was never entirely clear), but it seems this show isn’t exactly that, though Clark Gregg (Agent Coulson) might be involved.

We’ve learned to never say never when it comes to Marvel comebacks, so stay tuned for more news.

Release windows: October 2027 / October 2028 / October 2029 | Showrunner: Bryan Andrews | Cast: Unconfirmed

Following a four-episode season in 2025, the animated series Marvel Zombies will be returning for three consecutive years (starting in 2027) just in time for Halloween.

The twist is that we’re now looking at three two-part specials: The Winter Soldier, The Fist of Khonshu, and The Last Guardian. It’s a huge departure from the previous plans with the Zombies and What If...? series, but it's one that could pay off.

Nothing is known about these small-scale projects at this point, but we know Marvel Zombies was a large enough hit on Disney+ to warrant more, and we’re okay with being surprised when it comes to format.

One has to assume these specials could work as one-shot comic books, but it’ll be interesting to see whether Marvel Television can tell cohesive and compelling stories with even shorter story arcs.

Ticket sales for this year’s Dezeen Awards party, taking place on 16 November at the Royal Opera House in London, will close next week.

Tickets are available until 23:59 London time on 10 November, meaning there are just a few more days left to secure your place.

Book your tickets now before sales close.

An evening celebrating the world’s best architecture and design

Taking place at the Royal Opera House in London on 16 November, the party will bring together shortlisted studios, judges and the international architecture and design community to celebrate this year’s projects and reveal the 2026 winners.

Guests will begin the evening with a champagne reception provided by this year’s exclusive drinks partner, Veuve Clicquot.

The champagne house will serve its signature cuvée, recognisable by its distinctive yellow labels, which have become synonymous with the brand since 1877.

Earlier this year, the brand partnered with British-Nigerian designer Yinka Ilori to create a colourful installation at Milan design week to to mark the launch of his Chasing the Sun gift collection.

Later in the evening, British architect and 2026 judge Norman Foster will deliver a keynote speech that will focus on the future of architecture, drawing on his position as one of the most influential architects of his generation.

Guests will then watch the highly anticipated announcement of this year’s Dezeen Awards winners, with 392 shortlisted projects competing in 49 categories across architecture, interiors and design.

The winners have been selected by this year’s panel of industry-leading judges, following last week’s announcement of the separate public vote, in which readers selected their favourite projects to win.

Category winners announced on the evening will then go on to compete for the prestigious overall project of the year titles in architecture, interiors and design.

Tickets for the party are available at £349 per ticket. Studios that have entered this year’s Dezeen Awards are be eligible to purchase discounted tickets for £249, saving £100 per ticket.

Hundreds of architects and designers from across the globe including Japan, South Africa and the United States have already secured their tickets.

Tickets are available until 23:59 London time on 10 November. Secure your place now to avoid disappointment.

Avianca LifeMiles → Star Alliance Premium Cabin

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

Compare the best travel credit cards for maximizing points and miles.

New to travel rewards? Start here with our beginner-friendly guide.

Learn the fundamentals of earning and redeeming travel points.

Maximize free hotel stays with the right loyalty programs.

Fly for nearly free with smart airline mile strategies.

Build the perfect credit card portfolio for your spending habits.

Browse our complete library of travel rewards articles.

ChooseFI has partnered with CardRatings for our coverage of credit card products. ChooseFI and CardRatings may earn compensation from card issuers when a customer clicks on a link, when an application is approved, or when an account is opened. Opinions, reviews, analyses & recommendations are the author's alone, and have not been reviewed, endorsed or approved by any of these entities. American Express is a ChooseFI advertiser.

Free calculators, a personalized FI roadmap, and a community of 25K+ on the same journey.

One of the cheapest ways to fly Star Alliance business and first class

Picture this: you settle into a Lufthansa Boeing 747-8 business class seat on the upper deck, somewhere over the Atlantic, a glass of Champagne in hand, your fully flat bed already made up behind you. New York to Frankfurt — a route that commands $4,000 to $8,000 in cash for a business class ticket — and you're here for 63,000 Avianca LifeMiles. No fuel surcharges. No carrier-imposed fees eating into your redemption. Just the experience. That's the moment the FI community lives for. Not the luxury itself — though Lufthansa's business class product is genuinely exceptional — but the *math*. The realization that the loyalty system has a pricing gap so wide you could fly a 747 through it. Here's what the numbers actually mean. At the low end, a $4,000 business class ticket redeemed for 63,000 points works out to **6.3 cents per point (CPP)**. At the high end — peak travel season, last-minute pricing, transatlantic demand — that same seat hits $8,000 in cash, which means your LifeMiles are delivering **12.7 cents per point**. For context, most travel rewards experts consider 1–2 CPP a baseline for points value. You're achieving 6 to 12 times that baseline. The reason this feels like a genuine inefficiency rather than just a deal: Avianca LifeMiles prices Star Alliance awards on a **fixed award chart** that doesn't fluctuate with cash fare demand. When airlines raise transatlantic business class fares during summer holidays or major events, your points cost stays exactly the same. The gap between what the market charges and what LifeMiles charges simply grows wider. And then there's the no-fuel-surcharge policy — the detail that separates LifeMiles from competitors like British Airways Avios, which can pile $600+ in carrier surcharges onto a single redemption. With LifeMiles, you pay government taxes only, typically $50–$150 for a transatlantic itinerary. That's it. For the ChooseFI community, this redemption represents exactly the kind of asymmetric value that underpins a great travel rewards strategy: use points where cash prices are highest, eliminate fees wherever possible, and fly a product you'd never justify paying full price for — because you didn't have to.

The core insight here is understanding why Avianca LifeMiles prices Star Alliance awards so aggressively — and then understanding how to get points into the program quickly enough to actually use them.

Avianca is a Star Alliance member, which means it has reciprocal award booking agreements with every other Star Alliance carrier — United, Lufthansa, Swiss, ANA, EVA Air, Singapore Airlines, and more. Like all alliance programs, LifeMiles can sell award seats on partner airlines. The pricing is set by Avianca's own award chart, not by Lufthansa's chart or United's chart.

This is the pricing inefficiency. Lufthansa's own frequent flyer program (Miles & More) charges over 100,000 miles for the same transatlantic business class route and layers on fuel surcharges. United MileagePlus charges 70,000–88,000 miles for the same seat. Avianca LifeMiles charges 63,000 miles with zero carrier surcharges. Same seat. Same plane. Wildly different cost.

Avianca keeps rates competitive because they want their program to be attractive to non-Avianca flyers — it's a revenue play. And because their route network is South America-focused, they've built a powerful award program that earns loyalty from customers who may never set foot on an Avianca plane.

Many programs claim no surcharges, then quietly pass through "carrier-imposed fees" that look indistinguishable from surcharges. LifeMiles is one of the genuine exceptions. On partner award bookings — including Lufthansa, Swiss, and other carriers that traditionally levy heavy surcharges — LifeMiles charges only government taxes. For a JFK–FRA business class award, expect to pay roughly $50–$150 out of pocket. That's the full cash outlay.

This is where the transfer partner ecosystem matters. LifeMiles accepts transfers from:

The instant Citi transfer is particularly powerful: if you find available award space today, you can move points and call LifeMiles to book within the same session. Capital One and Bilt are nearly as fast. Marriott's 3:1 ratio makes it less efficient unless you have a large Bonvoy balance and no better option.

LifeMiles runs frequent purchased miles sales, often 100%+ bonus miles on purchases. This is a legitimate strategy for topping off your balance when you're a few thousand miles short of a redemption. Combined with transfer partners, it creates significant flexibility. Watch for promotions at lifemiles.com — they typically appear 6–8 times per year.

The golden rule of any points transfer: never transfer miles until you've confirmed award availability. LifeMiles miles are non-refundable once transferred, and award space can disappear in hours.

United MileagePlus has one of the best Star Alliance partner award search tools available to the public. Go to united.com, start a search as if you're booking an award ticket, and look for business class availability on Lufthansa or Swiss flights on your target route. United displays Lufthansa partner space accurately in most cases. You're looking for "saver" level availability — marked as "X" class on Lufthansa.

Lufthansa's own website (lufthansa.com) sometimes shows availability that United misses, and vice versa. Search both. You're not booking here — just confirming the seat exists.

An ExpertFlyer subscription ($10/month) lets you set availability alerts for specific routes and dates. For a high-value redemption like this, it's often worth one month of the service to monitor your target window.

LifeMiles opens its booking window 355 days in advance. For peak travel periods — summer transatlantic, major European events — set a calendar reminder to search at the 355-day mark. Business class award space on Lufthansa releases in small batches and fills quickly.

Once you've confirmed space, act fast. Log into your points program and initiate the transfer:

Transfer exactly what you need — 63,000 miles for a one-way business class transatlantic award — plus a small buffer of 2,000–3,000 miles for potential routing variations.

Go to lifemiles.com and search your award. The website has improved but can still be inconsistent with partner availability display. If the online search doesn't show the space you found on United.com, call LifeMiles directly at their customer service line. Have your flight numbers, dates, and cabin class ready. Phone agents can access and book partner availability that the website sometimes fails to display.

After booking, you'll receive a confirmation email with a PNR (record locator). Verify this on the operating carrier's website — go to lufthansa.com or swiss.com and look up your reservation using the PNR. If it pulls up correctly, your booking is solid. Save this confirmation and check it once more 72 hours before departure.

Avianca LifeMiles uses a distance-based award chart for Star Alliance partner flights, and their chart is consistently 15-40% cheaper than competing programs for the same seats. The reason is strategic: Avianca wants to attract members to its loyalty program, and aggressive pricing on aspirational redemptions is how they compete with larger alliances. For you, this means the exact same Lufthansa First Class seat or ANA business class seat costs significantly fewer points when booked through LifeMiles versus through United MileagePlus or the carrier directly. The catch is that LifeMiles availability depends on the operating carrier releasing award space to partners, so you still need to search on the carrier or United.com before transferring.

Card links may earn ChooseFI a commission. See our advertiser disclosure.

Let's walk through a real redemption so you can see exactly what this looks like in practice.

The Route: New York JFK → Frankfurt FRA, nonstop The Aircraft: Lufthansa Boeing 747-8, upper deck business class The Cash Price: $6,500 (a realistic peak-season transatlantic business class fare) The Points Cost: 63,000 Avianca LifeMiles Out-of-Pocket Fees: ~$85 in government taxes CPP Achieved: 10.3 cents per point

You've transferred 63,000 Citi ThankYou points to LifeMiles — instantly — spotted the award space on United.com the morning it opened, called LifeMiles to confirm, and had your booking locked in within 90 minutes of finding the seat.

Lufthansa's 747-8 upper deck business class is one of the most iconic products in commercial aviation. The upper deck seats — in a 2-2-2 configuration — sit quieter and smoother than the main deck, with fewer passengers and a more intimate cabin feel.

Your seat converts to a fully flat bed of approximately 6 feet. You'll have direct aisle access (depending on your seat selection), a large personal entertainment screen, and Lufthansa's catering, which consistently ranks among the top transatlantic carriers. The meal service on JFK–FRA is legitimate: a proper multi-course dinner, a breakfast service as you approach Germany, and a snack menu available throughout.

Before you board, you'll have access to Lufthansa's business class lounge at JFK — typically the Lufthansa Business Lounge or, depending on timing, the partner lounge. Hot food, showers, and a genuine departure experience that costs nothing beyond the taxes you've already paid.

The flight itself is roughly 7.5 hours. In a flat bed, that's a real night's sleep — or close enough. You arrive in Frankfurt rested, which matters whether you're connecting onward or starting your trip immediately.

For the ChooseFI community: this is the redemption that pays for the honeymoon, the milestone anniversary trip, or the family adventure you've been planning since you first discovered travel rewards. The $6,500 you didn't spend stays in your index funds.

ChooseFI has partnered with CardRatings for our coverage of credit card products. ChooseFI and CardRatings may receive a commission from card issuers.

Book at the 355-day mark for peak dates. Lufthansa releases a small number of partner award seats when the schedule opens. The travelers who get summer business class awards are almost always the ones who searched on day 355. Set a calendar alert. Be ready.

Use Citi ThankYou for time-sensitive bookings. Because Citi transfers to LifeMiles are instant, you can find award space and complete your booking in a single session. This matters when you're racing to secure a specific seat before someone else grabs it.

Watch for LifeMiles purchased miles promotions. If you're 10,000–15,000 miles short of a redemption, purchasing miles during a 100% bonus promotion can effectively cost you 0.5–0.7 cents per mile — still leaving you with excellent overall CPP on the redemption. Check lifemiles.com regularly or set a Google Alert for "LifeMiles promotion."

Verify your booking on the operating carrier's website. After booking through LifeMiles, always look up your PNR on lufthansa.com or swiss.com. If it appears correctly, your seat is real. If it doesn't, call LifeMiles immediately.

Search Swiss as an alternative to Lufthansa. Swiss International Air Lines is also a Star Alliance member, also bookable through LifeMiles at the same rates, and also operates excellent business class products on transatlantic routes. Swiss often has more partner award availability than Lufthansa on certain dates.

Never transfer points before confirming availability. This is the most common — and most expensive — mistake in travel rewards. LifeMiles does not allow re-transfers out of the program.

Don't ignore the miles expiry policy. LifeMiles expire 12 months from your last earning activity. Any account activity — earning miles, purchasing miles, even a credit card transaction — resets the clock on your entire balance. Set a recurring reminder every 10 months to run a small transaction if you're holding a balance without an imminent booking.

Don't book Lufthansa First Class expecting the same experience. Lufthansa's First Class is a separate product with separate availability and pricing. Business class is the sweet spot here — outstanding value, more seats, and significantly better availability.

Avoid mixing Marriott Bonvoy transfers with tight timelines. At 3:1 and 1–5 days transfer time, Bonvoy is the slowest and least efficient path into LifeMiles. Plan accordingly.

This redemption is built for the experienced FI traveler who has already earned their first free night or domestic flight and is ready to graduate to aspirational international business class. You don't need to fly business class constantly — but for a honeymoon, a milestone birthday, or a long-planned European trip, this is the redemption that makes the experience extraordinary without derailing your savings rate.

You need 63,000 LifeMiles per person, per direction. A couple flying one-way needs 126,000 miles. Most people will get there through a combination of one or two travel credit card welcome bonuses and organic spending — entirely achievable within the FI community's existing card strategy.

## Rated: Hard — Here's Why This redemption earns its **hard** rating from three compounding challenges, none of which is insurmountable but all of which require deliberate effort. **Challenge 1: Award Availability Is Limited.** Lufthansa releases partner award seats conservatively. You may search for weeks before finding space on your preferred route and dates. This isn't a redemption where you decide Tuesday you want to go and book Thursday. You need a booking window of weeks to months, and flexibility in your travel dates helps enormously. **Challenge 2: The Booking Process Has Friction.** LifeMiles' website doesn't always surface partner award availability accurately. You'll likely need to search on United.com to find the space, then call LifeMiles to actually book it. That phone call requires patience — hold times vary, and you'll need to clearly communicate flight numbers, dates, and cabin class to the agent. **Challenge 3: Timing the Transfer Is High-Stakes.** Because you can't transfer points *out* of LifeMiles, you're committing your points the moment you initiate a transfer. Finding the seat, moving the miles, and completing the booking requires confidence and speed. ## How to Overcome the Challenges 1. **Start searching early** — 300+ days out for peak travel. Use ExpertFlyer alerts to monitor availability passively. 2. **Use Citi ThankYou for instant transfers** so you can act immediately when you find a seat. 3. **Practice the phone booking process** once before your high-stakes redemption — call LifeMiles with a simple question to understand their system. If you've successfully booked one or two simpler awards before, you can absolutely do this. The ChooseFI community has members completing this redemption every year — and every single one says the boarding moment made the effort completely worth it.

United shows Star Alliance partner availability that LifeMiles can book. Search for Saver-level awards in business or first class on your target route. Note the exact carrier, flight number, and date.

Log into LifeMiles.com and run the same search. Confirm the flight appears and note the exact mileage required. Some partner space visible on United may not be available through LifeMiles, so always double-check.

Amex MR, Chase UR, Citi ThankYou, and Capital One miles all transfer to LifeMiles. Amex transfers are often fastest (same day). Transfer only after confirming availability in Step 2.

Premium cabin award space is limited and competitive. Once your transferred miles appear in your LifeMiles account, complete the booking immediately. Do not wait — the seat can be taken by another searcher.

LifeMiles charges low taxes and fees on most partner awards, typically $30-$100 depending on the route. Some carriers (notably British Airways) add fuel surcharges, but most Star Alliance carriers keep fees minimal through LifeMiles.

Yes, Lufthansa First Class is bookable through LifeMiles at 87,000 miles one-way from the US to Europe. Availability is limited and typically released at 14 days before departure for partner programs, though some space appears further out. Search on United.com first to confirm Lufthansa First saver space exists before transferring.

Transfer times vary by partner. Amex Membership Rewards transfers often post same-day. Chase Ultimate Rewards transfers typically take 1-2 business days. Citi ThankYou and Capital One transfers can take 1-3 days. Always account for transfer time when booking time-sensitive award space.

Most Star Alliance carriers booked through LifeMiles have low or no fuel surcharges. The notable exception is British Airways (a oneworld carrier, not bookable through LifeMiles). Taxes and fees on typical Star Alliance routes run $30-$100 per ticket, making LifeMiles one of the cheapest programs for out-of-pocket award costs.

If the booking fails because award space disappeared between your search and transfer, the miles sit in your LifeMiles account. They do not expire as long as you have account activity every 12 months. You can use them for a future booking, but you cannot transfer them back to your original program.

Avianca LifeMiles is the program that lets FI-community travelers fly the same premium cabins as frequent business travelers at a fraction of the points cost. By consistently underpricing Star Alliance awards, LifeMiles turns 63,000-87,000 transferable points into experiences worth $8,000 or more. The process requires discipline (never transfer before confirming availability), patience (searching for saver space takes time), and a willingness to navigate a less polished booking experience. But the value is real: 15-20 cents per point on aspirational flights that would be unreachable at cash prices.

Liverpool and Newcastle United are reportedly keeping tabs on Sunderland midfielder Noah Sadiki ahead of the January transfer window, with several European heavyweights also interested in the highly rated youngster.

According to a report from Hooligan Soccer, the 21-year-old has attracted significant attention following his impressive performances for the Black Cats, with clubs across England and Europe monitoring his development.

Liverpool are understood to be considering a move for the DR Congo international as they look to add more energy and quality to their midfield department. His ability to contribute defensively while progressing the ball could make him an attractive option for the Merseyside outfit.

Newcastle United are also keeping a close eye on his situation, while Juventus, Napoli and Atletico Madrid have reportedly registered an interest in the midfielder.

Chelsea and Manchester United have also been linked with the Sunderland star, highlighting his growing reputation in European football.

Sadiki could prove to be a useful long-term addition for Liverpool. The youngster is comfortable in central and defensive midfield, and his versatility lets him fill in at right-back when needed.

Want more CaughtOffside coverage? Add us as a preferred source on Google to your favourites list for news you can trust.

However, convincing Sunderland to part ways with their prized asset will not be straightforward. The Championship promotion winners secured his services from Union Saint-Gilloise in 2025 and gave him a five-year contract running until 2030.

The report claims that Sunderland could demand between €55m and €60m to sanction his departure, with performance-related bonuses and a sell-on percentage also potentially forming part of their demands.

Sadiki has featured regularly this season, starting 11 of his 12 appearances across competitions. He also made 35 appearances last term and has established himself as an important squad member.

Liverpool could certainly benefit from his energy, versatility and ability to win possession. However, it remains to be seen whether they are prepared to meet Sunderland’s valuation when the transfer window opens in January.

Dakota Johnson and Machine Gun Kelly’s Full Relationship Timeline

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

Dakota Johnson and Machine Gun Kelly have yet to define their relationship publicly, but the two stars have been spending plenty of time together.

The actress and musician were first linked in September 2026, when they were photographed together in New York City. Days later, a source told People that Johnson and Kelly, whose real name is Colson Baker, “have been hanging out and seeing each other under the radar for months now.”

The two stars had known each other before, but according to the source, they became closer after attending Taylor Swift and Travis Kelce’s wedding in July.

Since then, Johnson has watched Kelly walk the runway in Milan, and sources have shared more about how their connection developed.

Below, everything we know about Johnson and Kelly’s rumored relationship so far.

Johnson and Kelly both attended Taylor Swift and Travis Kelce’s July 3 wedding at Madison Square Garden in New York City.

The two stars already knew each other, but a source close to Kelly told People in September that they “hit it off” at the wedding.

On September 28, a source gave Entertainment Tonight similar intel, telling the outlet that Johnson and Kelly “really connected at Taylor and Travis’s wedding.”

On September 12, Johnson and Kelly were photographed together in New York City, getting out of the same SUV and entering Swift’s Tribeca apartment.

Four days later, a source close to Kelly told People that Kelly and Johnson “have been hanging out and seeing each other under the radar for months now.”

The source explained that Johnson and Kelly “started off as friends and were texting often, but now they’re spending more time together.”

“It’s a very new start, but they’re smitten with each other right now,” the insider added. “They haven’t put a label on their relationship yet. They are just seeing where spending time together will take them.”

A second source characterized things as “super casual” at that point.

On September 22, Johnson and Kelly both traveled to Italy for Milan Fashion Week and attended the same event.

Three days later, Johnson addressed the broader attention her dating life receives during an appearance on Vogue’s The Run-Through podcast, though she never mentioned Kelly specifically.

“Even if I go to dinner with my manager or my lawyer, it’s like, ‘Seen with mystery man,’” she said. “I also don’t care.”

When asked whether she ever feels compelled to correct speculation about her personal life, Johnson explained why she prefers not to engage with it.

“I think it’s just futile. People are gonna believe what they wanna believe, and I don’t have the energy to fight for that,” she said. “I would much rather focus on other things in my life. Every day, there’s something on the internet that’s ridiculous and true or not true, and I don’t care.”

On September 28, a source shared more about Kelly and Johnson’s relationship with Entertainment Tonight: “Dakota was the one who made the first move. He’s very shy around her, but he likes her a lot. It’s still new, but they’re having fun and seeing where things go.”

The insider added, “Dakota has been a fan of his music and has always found him cute. He likes her a lot, too.”

On October 19, a source close to Kelly told People that he and Johnson were spending more time together. “They have been out on dates that people don’t know about because there aren’t any photos.”

The insider said that they’re in the “dating phase,” and “aren’t ready to go official yet.”

“They both really like each other and have been seeing each other more frequently,” the source shared. “They haven’t put a label on their relationship yet, but they’re growing closer every time they hang out.”

In the past few months, they’ve especially connected over their “same dry and dark sense of humor,” the source said. “[They] make each other laugh.”

Days later, Kelly and Johnson were photographed out in Los Angeles on October 23. They first left a music studio together, riding off in Kelly’s red Maserati. That evening, they were seen together again, leaving the Chateau Marmont.

A source close to Johnson gave People an update on their dynamic on October 28. “She’s happy and enjoys spending time with him,” the insider said. “There’s a lot of chemistry and she’s been going to the studio with him while he works. For now, it makes her happy.”

On November 3, Kelly and Johnson were photographed leaving a restaurant in Calabasas, California, after a dinner date. They were smiling and talking to each other in the shots.

A Seagram Heir, a Bankrupt Hedge Funder, and More: Consignors for the November Marquee Sales Revealed

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

By Daniel Cassady, Harrison Jacobs, Alex Greenberger, Brian Boucher November 4, 2026 3:56pm

What a difference a year makes. The marquee New York sales return in just under two weeks, and while galleries still seem to be on a market seesaw, the auction houses have found a new, lucrative normal. When in doubt, just sell A+ material by established blue-chip artists with impeccable provenance. Sounds simple, right?

One suspects the auction houses’ specialists and rainmakers have found the task easier after a solid season last November, topped by Sotheby’s record-shattering sale of Gustav Klimt‘s Portrait of Elisabeth Lederer (1914–16) for $236.4 million. May was even more impressive: sales topped $2.5 billion across Christie’s, Sotheby’s, and Phillips, roughly double the $1.3 billion the three houses took in during May 2025. Related Articles Guillaume Cerutti to Lead the Château de Versailles After Short Stint at Pinault Collection Christie's First Fine Art Auction in Abu Dhabi Totals $8.94 M.

Provenance always matters in the art market, but in a more selective environment it has become essential to the success of each house’s sales. So much so that we seem to have moved from a time when each house had one or two single-owner mega-sales each season—like say, the $1.5 billion Paul Allen sale in 2022—to one in which a hefty share of the lots in each sale carry a named consignor.

To wit, 40 of the 79 lots in Christie’s 20th Century Evening Sale have named consignors, and that doesn’t include the seven lots carrying the Heroes & Lovers label, which it is an open secret were consigned by artist Jeff Koons. In Christie’s 21st Century Evening Sale, 35 of the 58 lots have a named consignor. Over at Sotheby’s, 22 of the 53 lots in The Now and Contemporary Evening Auction have a named consignor. The trend is less pronounced in the Modern Evening Auction, where only 13 of the 50 lots have a named consignor. But perhaps that’s because another 11 lots come from a single private American collection billed as “Shaping Modernity.” More on that one in a minute.

So why are there so many big-name collections coming to auction? Part of it is just timing, according to art adviser Meredith Darrow. Several major estates have come to market over the past few auction seasons, and auction houses have always been happy to put a famous collector’s name on a sale. But there’s another reason to make more noise about provenance these days. With the auction market still trying to recover from its post-Covid slump, a well-known name can help drum up interest and, potentially, push prices higher.

“Auction houses will do anything they can to promote the value of the works,” Darrow told ARTnews. “Calling out a notable provenance would and should increase interest and value of the work.”

It would be reasonable to assume, then, with all the named collectors, that there is little left for your favorite art sleuths to dig up in the paper trail of catalogues raisonnés, exhibition histories, and old press clippings. That would be wrong. There was no shortage of provenance breadcrumbs among this season’s lots, and ARTnews is happy to share them.

First off, as we reported last week, the cover lot of Christie’s 20th Century Evening Sale, Wassily Kandinsky’s Betonte Ecken (1922–23), was consigned by the Nahmads, the prominent art-dealing and collecting dynasty. It carries an estimate of $60–$80 million.

The same sale includes an untitled 1968 acrylic on paper by Mark Rothko (estimate $10–$15 million), which appears to have been consigned by Ed Rabin, the former president of Hyatt Hotels, and his wife, Jackie. The work, two crimson fields hovering over a scarlet ground, last sold in 1997, and a 2023 catalogue lists it as being in the Rabins’ collection.

Two lots in the sale carry the label “Surrealist Masterpieces from an Important American Collection,” and both point to Clarissa and Edgar Bronfman Jr. Edgar is a billionaire business executive and an heir to the Seagram fortune. The first, Wifredo Lam’s 1943 work Ogue Orisa (Eggue Orissa, l’herbe des dieux), estimated at $10–$15 million, appeared in MoMA’s recent Lam retrospective, where the Bronfmans were listed as lenders. The second, Roberto Matta’s Science, conscience et patience du vitreur (1944), carries an estimate of $5–$7 million. It also appears in a 2016 Architectural Digest feature on the couple’s penthouse.

The pickings were slimmer in Christie’s 21st Century Evening Sale. That’s partly because the works sold more recently, and partly because, as noted above, 60 percent of the lots already have a listed consignor. Still, we found that Ed Ruscha’s Male and Female (1987), estimated at $4–$6 million, was consigned by architect and real estate developer William Ehrlich and Ruth Lloyds. According to the provenance, they bought it directly from the artist, and Robert Dean and Erin Wright’s 2007 catalogue raisonné lists it in their collection.

Meanwhile, Vija Celmins’s Night Sky #21 (2009–13), estimated at $3–$5 million, appears to have been consigned by the Roman family, at least according to the catalogue for the Fondation Beyeler’s Celmins show last year. That appears to be Emannuel Roman, the CEO of investment firm PIMCO.

Back to Sotheby’s: in addition to the 11 lots in the Modern Evening Auction, one lot in The Now and Contemporary Evening Auction carries the “Shaping Modernity” label. A source with knowledge of the consignment told ARTnews that the consignor behind the group is George Weiss, the hedge fund founder now in bankruptcy. As Bloomberg reported in June, Weiss asked a Florida bankruptcy judge for permission to sell a 1972 self-portrait by Francis Bacon. Last month, Bloomberg reported that Weiss had struck a deal with Sotheby’s to sell more than a dozen works to repay a Bank of America loan. The Bacon will now be offered in The Now and Contemporary Evening Auction with an estimate of $10–$15 million. Additional Highlights include Paul Signac’s Collioure. Les Balancelles (Opus 167) (estimate $12–$18 million), Camille Pissarro’s Hameau aux environs de Pontoise (estimate $2.5–$3.5 million), Henri Matisse’s La Jetée à Collioure (estimate $5–$7 million), and Paul Cézanne’s L’Oncle Dominique (estimate $3–$5 million).

Two Agnes Martin works in The Now and Contemporary Evening Auction, Innocence (1999) and Untitled #2 (1991), were last exhibited at the Froehlich Foundation (Stiftung Froehlich). That would be the private museum outside Stuttgart, Germany, that was built to house the collection of ARTnews Top 200 Collectors Anna and Josef Froehlich. Martin’s catalogue raisonné lists the foundation as the owner of Innocence, and both works carry the label “Property from a Distinguished European Collection.” Connect the dots.

Then there’s Salman Toor’s Lavender Boy (estimate $60,000–$80,000). In a 2021 interview with Larry’s List, Rob and Eric Thomas-Suwall, the collectors behind the Instagram account @theicygays, named the Toor as the most treasured piece in their collection.

If you’re wondering which “Mid-Atlantic Institution” is deaccessioning its untitled Lee Bontecou sculpture, you need only look at the exhibition history. The work, estimated at $1.2–$1.8 million, has had just one owner and has been on view at Bryn Athyn College in Pennsylvania for the past 13 years.

Speaking of deaccessioning: Keith Haring’s portrait of Grace Jones (estimate $3.5–$5 million) was last exhibited at Museum MACAN in Jakarta, Indonesia, from 2021 to 2022. The museum’s 2021 exhibition guide lists the work as part of its collection. But as the Art Newspaper noted when the private museum opened in 2017, most of its collection comes from the holdings of its founder, Top 200 Collector Haryanto Adikoesoemo. So who the actual consignor is remains a little fuzzy.

Lastly, four works in Sotheby’s Modern Evening Auction and one in The Now and Contemporary Evening Auction carry the label “Magnum Opus: A Private Collection of Exceptional Art and Objects Through the Ages.” Which collector, pray tell, is behind such a weighty title? Edgar Degas’s 1882 drawing Danseuse rajustant son chausson proved to be the key to this particular treasure chest. According to Michel Schulman’s digital catalogue raisonné of Degas’s work, the drawing belongs to the Frederick Iseman Art Trust. The trust is also credited with the work in the catalogue for MoMA’s 2016 exhibition “Edgar Degas: A Strange New Beauty.”

The collection points to private equity investor Frederick Iseman and includes works by Georges Seurat, Pablo Picasso, Max Ernst, and David Smith in this month’s evening sales. Iseman, who founded CI Capital Partners in 1993, is also Helen Frankenthaler’s nephew and a former president of her foundation. He made headlines in 2023 when he sued his fellow board members, accusing them of exploiting his aunt’s legacy for their own benefit. The dispute turned particularly nasty, with accusations flying in both directions, before a New York judge dismissed the case on procedural grounds in September 2024 without ruling on the allegations themselves.

For all the attention Sotheby’s has lavished on the “Magnum Opus” collection, Iseman’s name appears nowhere in its marketing. And there has been plenty of attention: the collection numbers more than 900 objects, ranging from ancient Roman sculpture and old master paintings to Impressionist art and 20th-century design. More than 600 of them were offered across four dedicated sales in October, with a combined presale estimate of more than $60 million. Sotheby’s even took over four floors of the Breuer building for a public exhibition ahead of the auctions.

(And, as usual, spokespeople for Christie’s and Sotheby’s told ARTnews that they cannot comment on the identities of consignors.)

Hey, Siri: Plug into my app like you said you'd do way back in 2024

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

More than two years after promising it, Apple lets developers test Siri AI app integration

As of Tuesday, developers can explore expanded Siri AI integration for their Apple platform apps through beta releases of iOS 27.3, iPadOS 27.3, macOS 27.3, and visionOS 27.3. Next spring, nearly three years after announcing plans for deeper third-party Siri integration, Apple expects to begin accepting App Store submissions using the new capabilities.

"This developer beta offers a sneak peek at Siri AI app intents for developers," Apple said in a developer news post. "With this early Developer Preview, your app can work directly with Siri AI, giving you a first look at new ways to reach and serve your customers."

Anything is likely to be an improvement. Apple's Siri, a voice-activated assistant service, dates back to 2011 and gained limited third-party app access in 2016. In 2024, it became an interface for interacting with Apple Intelligence, its suite of generative AI features.

Apple Intelligence has not been very intelligent – several advertised Siri capabilities were delayed, so much so that Apple was sued in 2025 over allegations of misleading marketing. After a shakeup of Apple's machine learning group in December 2025, Siri and Apple Intelligence received a makeover at Apple's Worldwide Developers Conference in June.

"Before this year, Siri was a timer with a voice," said Roberto Gutierrez, Apple platforms staff engineer at a media company and author of the e-book On-Device Intelligence, in an email to The Register. "Weather, alarms, calls. Anything past that was a coin toss.

"On the developer side, SiriKit covered a handful of domains and Shortcuts only worked if users memorized the phrase. Siri AI is the first version that understands context and can chain actions across apps. That's a real change."

There is a catch, however, Gutierrez observed. Siri AI is available in a limited number of languages and regions. Support for Traditional Chinese is expected by the end of 2026, and EU availability remains a matter of negotiation – Siri AI is unavailable on iOS, iPadOS, and watchOS in the bloc as Apple works through interoperability concerns under Europe's Digital Markets Act.

"Apple is working constructively with the EU in an attempt to find a path forward," the company said last month without committing to any concessions.

Gutierrez said that he expects opening Siri to third-party developers will be useful to some extent.

"Siri can only do what apps expose through App Intents, and SiriKit is on its way out," he said. "If your app's main actions aren't exposed, the new Siri can't see you. The bigger shift is letting Claude, Gemini and others plug in. Developers already choose between three options for every AI feature: Apple's system model, an open model through MLX, or a cloud API. Siri is heading the same way. It doesn't need to be the smartest model. It needs to be the best router. That's the right call."

Apple has made choosing between those three options more difficult by failing to publish benchmarks that show how its Foundation Models compare with open-weight alternatives.

The biz has published measurements of how its third-generation Foundation Models compare to their 2025 predecessors. But the company's commitment – to "[share] more details on our latest foundation models, including updated evaluations and benchmarks, in a technical report later this summer" – remains unfulfilled so far as we're aware.

Gutierrez ran his own benchmarks to compare Apple's iOS system model (FM system, ~3B) to several open-weight models that are suitable for use on-device.

"On an A17 Pro iPhone running iOS 26, I tested Apple's system model against Qwen3 1.7B and 4B, Llama 3.2 3B and Gemma 3 4B on a structured-output task," he said. "Speed: Apple's model ran at about 149 tok/s, roughly 3x the fastest open model (Qwen3 1.7B, about 46)."

Quality, he said, was a tie, with every model passing five typed checks. But memory, he said, was where Apple's models had an advantage.

"The system model's weights live outside your app's memory budget," he explained. "My app peaked at 12 MB during inference. An open 4B model costs 2.4-2.7 GB, and a 2.4 GB model plus your app plus a WebView is a crash report. So yes, it's competitive, and on iPhone it's the default I'd recommend. If you want your own model, Qwen3 1.7B is the one."

But Gutierrez urged Apple to publish proper competitive benchmarks. "Their numbers compare against their own previous models," he said. "Developers aren't choosing between Apple 2025 and Apple 2026. We're choosing between Apple, Qwen and a cloud API. I had to build my own harness to answer a question Apple could settle with one table."

The absence of competitive model metrics is not the only gap. Apple has also failed to accommodate web apps.

"Web apps are locked out," said Gutierrez. "Foundation Models is a Swift API (and now Python). No JavaScript, no WebKit. A PWA's only option is to ship its own model into the browser and pay the full memory bill inside a WebView."

And bringing your own model may pose performance problems. "On an A17 Pro iPhone, the 3-4B open models pushed the phone into 'fair' thermal state and dropped to 12-24 tok/s," he said. "In practice, your own model on iPhone means ~2B or smaller."

With Siri AI unavailable on iPhones, iPads, and Apple Watches in the EU (and its rollout in mainland China subject to regulatory requirements), developers may need a fallback for global apps to handle regional restrictions. Apple's Private Cloud Compute offers eligible developers access to Foundation Models, Gutierrez observed, though it requires membership in the App Store Small Business Program, fewer than two million first-time App Store downloads across their apps, and the necessary entitlement. ®

All About Bonnie-Chance Roberts, Beanie Feldstein’s Wife

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

Beanie Feldstein just shared a major life update: She and her wife, Bonnie-Chance Roberts, quietly welcomed their first child. “She is genuinely beyond my wildest dreams. I really did dream her,” Feldstein told People of their baby girl in November 2026.

In May, the actress announced her pregnancy on Instagram, writing, “Limited Edition Scouse Beanie Baby coming soon!! 🎀🍼.”

The announcement comes nearly two years after the couple married in a camp-themed celebration in upstate New York.

Feldstein often keeps the spotlight on her work, but when it comes to Roberts, she doesn’t hold back. Over the years, she’s shared glimpses into their relationship, from how they met on set to building a life together.

So who is Bonnie-Chance Roberts? Here’s everything to know about Feldstein’s wife and their life as parents.

Originally from Liverpool, Roberts works behind the scenes in the entertainment industry as a producer.

Her credits include projects like Mary Queen of Scots, Bridget Jones’s Baby, and How to Build a Girl—which she worked on as a producer while Feldstein starred in it.

Feldstein and Roberts first connected while working on How to Build a Girl. Feldstein told Vogue in June 2023 that they initially met over Skype during the casting process. “Bon had been developing Caitlin Moran’s book How to Build a Girl into a movie for over five years when they finally started the casting process for Caitlin’s fictional proxy, Johanna, a 16-year-old girl from Wolverhampton, England,” Feldstein said. “With a dash of luck and a push from the universe, they ended up being open to meeting a Jewish girl from Los Angeles.”

Their working relationship quickly shifted. As Feldstein recalled to Vogue, “From the moment we first kissed, we both knew we would get married and months after that, Bon had announced that when the time came, she wanted to be the one to propose to me.”

The relationship grew steadily from there. By 2020, Feldstein publicly celebrated Roberts on Instagram, writing in a birthday tribute, “I’m pretty convinced you’re actually made of magic. The girl with the warmest heart, the most expansive imagination, and the most exceptional brain. I love you so much. Happy birthday, bubba!!!”

The couple didn’t keep things private for long. In September 2019, they stepped out together at the Toronto International Film Festival for the premiere of How to Build a Girl, marking their first red carpet appearance as a couple.

Roberts later posted about the experience on Instagram, writing, “They say it takes a village to build a girl, and they are right!”

Roberts took the lead when it came time to propose. Feldstein announced their engagement in June 2022, sharing photos from the moment on Instagram and captioning them, “I do, Bon.”

Roberts later described designing the ring during a long period when the couple lived apart due to the pandemic. “The dream was for the ring to look as if a shard of magic was frozen in time,” she told Vogue in 2023.

The couple tied the knot on May 20, 2023, at Cedar Lakes Estate in New York’s Hudson Valley.

The theme felt personal. Feldstein told Vogue, “I grew up going to summer camp for 10 years, and my parents and both sets of my grandparents met at summer camp, so camp is a lineage of love through the generations of my family. Even though we met in London and fell in love on a film set, to get married at a camp was a truly beautiful emotional homecoming.”

Roberts wore a tailored bridal suit for the ceremony, while Feldstein opted for a vintage-inspired Gucci gown.

Looking back on the day, Feldstein shared on Late Night with Seth Meyers in February 2024, “[My wedding] went so swimmingly,” she said. “Like, I’m such an anxious girly. And I was more calm at the wedding than I am, like, brushing my teeth, at the supermarket. I was so calm. I just got to look at her and hear her vows, and it was perfect.”

“We wrote our own vows, and it actually rained a little the day of our wedding, and we handwrote the vows and the trees sort of shook and dripped on my vows,” she continued. “And I was like, ‘Oh, I hope I know them by heart.’ But it was fine. It was actually all going perfectly.”

In May 2026, Feldstein and Roberts announced they were expecting their first child.

The couple shared the news with a joint Instagram post featuring a series of photos, including one of a cake decorated with pink bows and the message “B+B are having a baby!”

The announcement marked a new chapter for the couple—and a years-long dream realized for Feldstein. In 2021, the actress told Bustle, “Sixteen years from now I hope to be living in New York City or London, married to my girlfriend, and have babies and puppies running around while we both make movies, shows, and plays that feel meaningful and exciting and profound.”

Feldstein quietly welcomed her and Roberts’s baby girl. In a November 4 interview with People, she opened up about parenthood for the first time. She did not share her child’s name or birth date but marveled about life with her.

“She is genuinely beyond my wildest dreams. I really did dream her,” Feldstein said. “I always wanted to be a mom, and I always wanted a daughter. I look at her every day, and I’m like, ‘You’re just beyond anything I could have even tried to imagine.’”

“I love getting to watch her take in the world; it’s just the greatest gift. And getting to be a mom alongside my wife is the greatest gift,” she added. “She’s such a good mom, and it’s so special to watch. Motherhood is unbelievably beautiful.”

She reflected on one milestone she saw a few weeks before her interview. “There’s nothing like the first smile,” she said. “I could feel her soul from the moment she was born. I remember people told me newborns are little blobs and they don’t really come alive until 6 weeks. I disagree with that completely. I could feel her soul from the moment I met her, but when you get to see that externalized in the smile, it’s so magic.”

Feldstein doesn’t shy away from expressing how much Roberts means to her.

Speaking to People in February 2024, she said of married life, “My favorite part of married life is everything, but I think the big thing that felt different was saying ‘my wife.’ [It] has more power than like ‘my girlfriend’…you know, it’s like ‘my wife’ is like ‘oh, this is legit.’”

She added, “Oh my god, I just want her with me every second of every day.”

In her November 2026 interview with People, she touched on her gratitude raising a child with Roberts. “I don’t think anyone that has had a baby can look at their child and not just feel the most gratitude to the universe,” Feldstein said. “I feel very grateful every day that I get to be her mom and mother alongside my wife and watch her with my whole family.”
