Avoid California’s Crowded Trails With These 5 Quiet Coastline Hikes

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

There’s no shortage of spectacular hiking in California. Big Sur, Yosemite, and Redwoods have achieved international renown––and for good reason. But if you want to escape the crowds, the less congested San Luis Obispo coast offers scores of trails, uncrowded wilderness, and stellar vistas along Highway 1.

These routes invite you to hike on hills turned gold by spring flowers, spot seals in coastal reserves, and explore both long and short routes near cozy coastal towns—all without fighting for a parking spot at the trailhead. Integrate these hikes into a road trip along the iconic Highway 1, or just visit for an active getaway. San Luis Obispo’s landscapes are beautiful, the food is delicious, and, for the nights you’re not camping, there’s great lodging.

Named for the abundant yellow wildflowers that paint the hills gold every spring—namely bright yellow poppies, Deerbrush, and wild mustard—this unique park along the Morro Bay estuary is home to riparian environments, coastal scrub, and pines.

Scores of trails weave across 8,000 acres of wilderness and offer views of Morro Bay. But for epic views of the coast and a solid taste of all the park has to offer, start at the Hazard Peak Trailhead on Pecho Valley Road and hike out on Hazard Trail to the peak. From there, you can retrace your steps back to the junction with Canyon View Trail. Take that and descend a series of short switchbacks to Islay Creek Road, which will spit you out just down the street from the Hazard Peak Trailhead. You can also extend the hike by doing the full Hazard Peak loop, which is about 11 miles.

Located in the Lopez Lake Recreation Area outside Arroyo Grande, this trail climbs up and along the western ridge of the county park. Wildlife sightings of deer, birds, even black bears, aren’t uncommon. The last section of trail involves a steep climb to the peak, but the 360-degree views of the Oceano Dunes and coast to the west and surrounding Santa Lucia, Caliente, and Sierra Madre Mountains are worth the effort.

Start on Wittenberg Trail at the end of Lopez Dr. and branch off onto Duna Vista Loop Trail. Duna Vista Spur Trail will lead you to the peak. Hike directly back on the southernmost part of the loop for a slightly shorter route (8 miles) or split off onto the northern section for the full 8.8-mile trek. A day-use fee of $12 per vehicle is required.

This beautiful coastal walk isn’t a challenging one, but give yourself plenty of time to complete it: You’ll want to stop and soak in the wide ocean views, sea stacks, and wetlands. Most of the hike is along bluffs, but opportunities to descend and explore intertidal areas at low tide abound. Expect to see plenty of sea lions and bring your binoculars to try to spot an endangered snowy plover.

Start at the North Ocean parking lot on the edge of Cayucos and take Estero Bluffs Trail along the coast to the end of the park and back.

There’s more to this hike than classic California coastal scrub. In fact, as you climb over rolling hills, you’ll score views of Morro Bay and jagged Morro Rock, a volcanic plug in the estuary, and even drop into shady live oak groves. Volcanic peaks pepper the periphery, and opportunities to spot wildlife abound, including any of hundreds of bird species that follow the Pacific Flyway.

The state park’s trail system is extensive, but this route is a nice loop that touches a lot of the trails on the east side. Start at the parking lot on S Bay Blvd. and take Quarry Trail to Canet Trail, followed by Chumash, Crespi, Park Ridge, and Live Oak Trails. That leads you right back to the parking lot you started in. You can customize the length by combining various side trails, including to the top of Portola Trail, a small summit, which adds about a mile. A state park pass or $8 day-use fee is required.

This rugged trail leads into the Santa Lucia Wilderness within Los Padres National Forest, finally reaching an 80-foot waterfall that streams into an azure pool. Much of the trail slowly climbs through the woods alongside a creek and lush riparian zone in Big Falls Canyon. However, if you continue onward past the trio of falls, your efforts will be rewarded with sunny views of the surrounding Coast Ranges.

Start at Big Falls Trailhead on Upper Lopez Canyon Road and hike to the junction with Hi Mountain Trail. Return the same way. The last section of Lopez Canyon Road to the trailhead can be rough, so 4WD is recommended.

Eat: Gardens of Avila, located within Sycamore Mineral Springs Resort and Spa in San Luis Obispo, offers elegant dining, homegrown ingredients, and garden paths for after-dinner strolls.

Sip: Wineries abound. Oenophiles who appreciate organic and regenerative farming should visit Chamisal Vineyards in Edna Valley.

Explore Town: Los Osos offers welcoming coffee shops, local art galleries, and fine boutiques. Avila Beach is worth exploring for sunny waterfront vibes. Cayucos is home to classic seaside tourist scenes complete with souvenir shops and antique stores.

Campgrounds are in no short supply along Highway 1, and the San Luis Obispo area is no exception. Most of the state parks, like Montaña de Oro, Morro Bay, and Estero Bluffs, have their own developed campgrounds. Morro Beach also has a campground along the coast. Those can be booked online or in person, but they fill up quickly, especially on weekends. During the winter, these campgrounds are closed, so you’ll need to seek out dispersed camping or other lodging options.

Dispersed camping is strictly prohibited along Highway 1. However, some nearby mountain areas allow it. Check out TV Tower Road, Carrizo Plain National Monument, or Williams Hill Recreation Area.

Want to break up your nights outside? You have options. San Luis Obispo and nearby towns have plenty of hotels, BnB’s, and more luxurious accommodations. For a splurge, check out La Lomita Ranch, a chic and colorful former stable with painted tiles, terracotta, and horses in San Luis Obispo.

What the FTC Wants to Know About How Airlines Set Fares

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

Enter your email for a complimentary article + exclusive offers.

Airline pricing has come under more scrutiny in recent years after Delta disclosed it was using AI to price some fares and a lawsuit was filed against JetBlue, alleging that the company uses personal data to price airfares. The airline industry has said it uses no personal data to determine pricing.

Airline pricing is once again under scrutiny. Last week, the Federal Trade Commission ordered Delta Air Lines, United Airlines and JetBlue to provide data on how they set fares, as part of a study of whether companies use personal information to set prices.

The agency wants details on the companies’ pricing methods. The companies have 45 days to respond.

“We intend to scrutinize the opaque methodology that some companies in these industries may be using to determine how much consumers pay or how much gig workers are paid for the same product or service,” FTC Chairman Andrew Ferguson said in a statement.

The FTC sent similar orders to Lyft, DoorDash, Uber, Instacart, Target and Walmart, seeking data on pricing and, for the gig companies, on worker pay.

The Federal Trade Commission has ordered Delta Air Lines, United Airlines, and JetBlue to hand over data on how they set fares, part of a broader study into whether companies use personal information to price goods and services. The airlines have 45 days to respond. The same orders went to Lyft, DoorDash, Uber, Instacart, Target, and Walmart, with FTC Chairman Andrew Ferguson saying the agency intends to scrutinize "opaque" pricing methodology. The inquiry lands as fares have risen sharply this year, largely because of surging jet fuel costs, and as airlines expand their use of AI in pricing. Delta, which told investors in 2024 it was working with the Israeli startup Fetcherr and said last year it was pricing about 3% of domestic routes with AI with a goal of up to 25% by the end of 2025, said it does not use personal data to set fares and has never used, tested, or planned individualized pricing. United referred questions to Airlines for America, which said it welcomes the study and that its carriers do not engage in personalized pricing. JetBlue faces a class action filed in April alleging it uses trackers to set prices dynamically. Roughly a dozen states have proposed laws this year limiting how companies collect and use customer search data, with California, Arizona, Hawaii, Maryland, Pennsylvania, and Vermont proposing outright bans on surveillance pricing.

Mamdani's Five Ballot Questions Pass, But Arts Groups Remain Skeptical

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

Concerns over landmark sites and lack of public input have dampened trust in Proposal 3, in particular.

Two arts organizations remain concerned that one of New York City's approved ballot measures could weaken review processes over public land usage, leaving areas such as landmark sites vulnerable to hasty sales and without sufficient community input.

All five ballot measures passed with strong support in yesterday's midterms, though the Municipal Art Society (MAS) of New York and the New York Landmarks Conservancy objected to parts of Proposal 3, which expedites the review of eligible projects in three areas: street safety construction, the sale or leasing of city-owned property, and the acquisition of office space for city employees.

Both groups, along with organizations such as the League of Women's Voters, have expressed varying levels of agreement with its three bundled target areas and called for them to be considered separately for independent review.

The measure is one of five put forth by the Commission on Government Efficiency, a 15-member panel assembled by Mayor Zohran Mamdani. All items aim to streamline city operations through revising the City Charter, such as reducing city contractor paperwork and setting aside a rainy-day fund in case of emergency.

"New York has had a strong tradition of public participation and robust debate, and over time, that's been chipped away," Andrea Goldwyn, the director of public policy at New York Landmarks Conservancy, told Hyperallergic.

Goldwyn's primary concern is with the second prong of Proposal 3, which reduces public review when the city sells or transfers unused development rights at landmark sites. Originally, most sales or leases of city-owned property underwent a seven-month review process called the Uniform Land Use Review Process (ULURP) and required City Council's final approval.

Proposal 3 allows projects not believed to have a sizeable impact on the surrounding environment to bypass final City Council approval and go through a 90-day fast lane called the Expedited Land Use Review Procedure (ELURP). Projects approved for ELURP can be concurrently reviewed by community boards and borough presidents, and city-owned landmarked property can be sold without City Council approval. Undevelopable land can additionally be sold to owners of adjacent properties without a public auction.

The proposal also excuses the Department of Transportation (DOT) from notifying elected officials and community boards before starting major projects deemed necessary for safety, and can instead notify those stakeholders when construction commences. Consultations with the Police Department, Fire Department, the Mayor’s Office for People with Disabilities, and the Department of Small Business Services remain mandatory.

"We've gotten calls from people who see new projects going up in their neighborhoods that they didn't know about," Goldwyn continued. "I think we're going to be seeing more of that because people aren't going to have the kind of notification they're used to having."

The final part of the proposal removes hearing requirements and approvals from the City Council and the City Planning Commission about acquiring or leasing office space for city employees, instead leaving it solely to Yume Kitasei, the commissioner for the Department of Citywide Administrative Services. Local community boards and all five borough presidents must still be notified of each proposal.

In an earlier statement, MAS stated that trimming down these review processes "could make it easier to sell or transfer valuable public land and development rights without sufficient safeguards to ensure lasting public benefits."

MAS called for a stronger framework that determines how public lands can be used. Such recommendations include prioritizing land for parks, affordable housing, and other projects that serve the public, rather than selling or leasing land to parties with unclear interests.

"Streamlining government processes should not come at the expense of public accountability or lasting community benefits," the statement adds.

The Best Hot Dogs in Chicago, According to Someone Who Has Tried 'Em All

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

If you’re looking for the best hot dog in Chicago, you’ve come to the right place. While I can’t tell you how many hot dogs I’ve eaten in my life, I am sure that number is in the many thousands. After high school, I worked at Nick’s Nest, a hot dog stand in my Massachusetts hometown, where I would have one for lunch every day. They’re a favorite food to eat while traveling, and I regularly grab them in my daily life as well, because I live in Chicago, where the hot dog is a local staple.

Served in a steamed poppyseed bun, the Chicago dog has specific toppings: mustard, chopped onions, neon green relish, tomatoes (slices or wedges), pickles (usually spears, sometimes chips), spicy sport peppers, and celery salt. The all-beef dog has a natural casing, which gives a perfect snap when you bite into it. While most Chicago dogs are pretty good, the best ones are constructed so you taste everything in every bite. And you probably know this, but just to be sure: that bite will not include ketchup, as the tangy condiment doesn’t mesh with the other toppings (this is a real if-you-know-you-know thing in Chicago—and you want to be in the know). When you’re in the city, whether you’re siddling up to a hot dog stand or popping into a casual restaurant, order yours as a “Chicago dog” or ask for it “dragged through the garden”—a signal that you know what’s up, and that you want everything on it.

These are the best places to get a hot dog in Chicago, according to a dog-loving resident.

Read our complete Chicago travel guide here, which includes:

It feels very Chicago that you can get an excellent hot dog inside Home Depot. Fixin’ Franks is a family-owned spot with outposts in stores around the city. You’ll find the stands tucked near the exit, where it’s a treat for finishing all your home-repair shopping. There’s a tight, locally sourced menu of hot dogs, Polish and Maxwell Street sausages, Italian beef, and elotes. Get a classic Chicago dog—and if you want to push the envelope, add J.P. Graziano hot giardiniera on top.

1017 W. Irving Park Rd., Lakeview; 1701 W. Lawrence Ave., Ravenswood

While most restaurants follow the dragged-through-the-garden approach, Byron’s, which served their hot dogs at the White House during the Obama administration, takes a different approach. The standard dressing at the 51-year-old stand consists of mustard, relish, onions, tomato, pickle spears, and celery salt, and adds lettuce, green peppers, and cucumber slices (hot peppers are available on request). While this is, admittedly, a lot to maneuver while eating, the salad approach adds a lot of freshness and almost makes it seem like a healthy choice.

1250 S. Union Ave., University Village; 2775 N. Elston Ave., Avondale

Must order: Polish sausage sandwich, pork chop sandwich

Chicago’s hot dog stand culture includes more than just dogs. Jim’s is the best place for two other local beloveds: The Maxwell Street Polish and the pork chop sandwich. The Polish, a garlicky smoked beef and pork link, is generally credited to the stand’s namesake, Macedonian immigrant Jimmy Stefanovic, who took over his aunt and uncle’s stand in 1939. It’s served with mustard, tons of grilled onions, and sport peppers. The pork chop sandwich, served bone-in (bite carefully!) comes with the same garnishes, as does the all-beef hot dog. There’s no seating at the University Village location, which is open from 6 a.m. to 1 a.m., but you can eat at the metal counter or sit on the curb—Jim’s always feels like a party.

Must order: Hot dog, chocolate cake shake

When visitors come to town and want to try Chicago classics, I send them to Portillo’s. The local chain, which Dick Portillo founded in 1963, offers solid versions of local favorites, including hot dogs, Italian beef, and a Maxwell Street Polish. The steamed dog is expertly done and a go-to. Also a go-to is the chocolate cake shake, which is made with—you guessed it—a slice of chocolate cake blitzed into a milkshake. It’s decadently rich, and a true Chicago experience.

3057 N. Ashland Ave., Lakeview; 2449 W. Armitage Ave., Logan Square; 500 W. 35th St., Bridgeport; 4811 Dempster St., Skokie

Must-order: Hot dog and fries, RHR double cheeseburger

With four locations of this locally beloved stand (the O.G. Bridgeport location goes by 35th Street Red Hots), you are never far from one of the city’s best dogs. The links are served Depression-style, which means they have stripped-down toppings: just mustard, onion, relish, and sport peppers, with a pile of fries on top, for a full meal that tops out at just $5.20. Many locals, myself included, feel that Red Hot Ranch’s double smashburger is the best in the city, so get one of those to split as well.

6363 N. Milwaukee Ave., Norwood Park East; 333 S. Milwaukee Ave., Wheeling

In 1948, Maurie and Flaurie Berman opened their drive-in with two signatures. First, the couple appears as 12-foot hot dog statues on the building’s roof—you can’t miss this duo. And second, they serve a hot dog with a point of view: It’s a custom all-beef dog a bit larger than your standard link, and topped with mustard,

a pickle, sport peppers, and chopped onions, plus piccalilli and a pickled green tomato. It comes tucked with crinkle-cut fries in a custom cardboard box, which you can even peep in an episode of The Bear.

Must-order: Chicago-style Duck Inn Dog, fried cheese curds

What happens when you take a Chicago classic and put it in the hands of one of the city’s best chefs? You get Kevin Hickey’s Duck Inn Dog. Every element is made to Hickey’s specs: The juicy beef, pork, and duck fat sausage is grilled, tucked in a brioche poppyseed bun, and dressed with a “supercondiment” of mustard and relish mixed with tomato and onion. It’s finished with a dill pickle, pickled serranos, and celery salt (and perfect with fried cheese curds alongside). This is the glow-up the humble dog deserves.

Much of the love for 43-year-old The Wiener’s Circle is for its heckling servers, led by local institution Poochie Rollins. That experience is one reason to visit the Lincoln Park stand, which is famously open until 4 a.m. on weekends (and 2 a.m. the rest of the week). The other? The excellent food, like the char dog. Most stands steam their dogs, but the charring approach here adds a smoky flavor and crisper texture. Do not miss the fantastic cheese fries, with a generous dollop of tangy cheddar sauce.

Most hot dog stands in Chicago use locally made Vienna Beef links, and it’s worth venturing straight to the source to try one at this cafe, which is located across the street from the factory. Snag a red stool and order the textbook Chicago-style dog and a side of crispy fries. After lunch, peruse the goodies in the shop, which range from cases of hot dogs to hot dog stuffies.

Artur Wnorowski’s casual Polish joint serves some of my favorite sausages in town. All are made in-house: The kielbasa, which he learned to make from his grandfather, is smoked over pecan wood and dressed with caramelized onions, sport peppers, and hazy IPA mustard, while the all-beef dog is available Chicago-style or Polish-inspired. With sauerkraut, onions, sour cream, and a potato and cheese pierogi on top, it’s a nod to the sense of fun Chicago’s hot dog stands deliver.

We understand that time is the greatest luxury, which is why Condé Nast Traveler mines its network of experts and influencers so that you never waste a meal, a drink, or a hotel stay wherever you are in the world.

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

Cardano’s ADA could be approaching a crucial turning point, with one analyst suggesting its current chart structure bears striking similarities to the setup that preceded its historic 2,000% rally. However, before the next bull-market phase can take shape, ADA must reclaim two crucial price levels that could determine whether another explosive run is on the horizon.

In a recent X post, 360Trader shared Cardano’s weekly chart, highlighting similarities between ADA’s current market structure and the price action witnessed during its previous cycle. Despite the resemblance, the analyst believes the cryptocurrency has yet to confirm a bullish shift, noting that it remains stuck in a consolidation range and needs to reclaim key resistance levels.

360Trader identified $0.25 as the first hurdle to watch, explaining that a swift recovery above this mark would make ADA more interesting to traders. However, Cardano was still trading around $0.1997 at press time, per CoinGecko data, leaving it about 25.19% below the level.

The analyst also pointed to $1.37 as a much bigger milestone, describing it as the threshold that would signal the beginning of ADA’s next bull market phase. This means that even if Cardano manages to reclaim $0.25, it would still have considerable ground to cover before meeting the analyst’s conditions for a stronger bullish trend.

Drawing parallels with Cardano’s previous market cycle, 360Trader sees the possibility of another powerful upward move if the broader crypto market transitions into a bullish phase. The trader, however, stopped short of predicting how high ADA could climb, suggesting that investors may need to remain patient until the next altcoin rally gains momentum.

To put the historical comparison into perspective, a repeat of ADA’s 2,000% surge from its current price of $0.1997 would send the 18th-largest cryptocurrency to roughly $4.19. Such a move would take ADA beyond its former record high of $3.09.

One of Fall's Best New Colognes Was Inspired by... Gary, Indiana?

Fulton & Roark Cathedral of Ruins is a dark, sophisticated scent that’s perfect for the season.

Our product picks are editor-tested, expert-approved. We may earn a commission through links on our site. Why Trust Us?

AS SOMEONE WHO spent large chunks of his early twenties driving around the country pretending to be Jack Kerouac (while acting more like Hunter S. Thompson), I’ve spent time in just about every contiguous U.S. state. Some, as you might imagine, are more interesting than others—and Indiana isn’t exactly topping the list. So when I discovered one of my new favorite fall colognes was inspired by Gary, Indiana, I began questioning my own reality. But Fulton & Roark Cathedral of Ruins Extrait de Parfumis that good.

The only thing I know about Gary, Indiana is that it’s where Michael Jackson and the Jackson 5 were from—and they left. But after reading about the inspiration behind Fulton & Roark’s latest scent—and, more importantly, smelling it—I’m wondering if it might be worth a trip. Specifically, the fragrance is inspired by City Methodist Church in Gary, Indiana.

Built in the 1920s, the church is still admired for its grand Gothic architecture. Over the years, its roof caved in, but the stone walls remained intact, allowing vegetation to grow throughout its halls. Equal parts creepy and beautiful, it’s exactly the type of place I’d like to sneak into to see if I could spend a night without freaking out.

More so, its mysterious, earthy, and intriguing yet inviting atmosphere captures exactly the type of vibe I look for in cologne this time of year. After wearing the fragrance for over a month, here’s how it lived up to its inspiration.

Cathedral of Ruins opens with an intense combination of cypress, black peppercorn, and rose, before heart notes of saffron and Peru balsam begin to emerge. After 20 minutes or so, the base notes of leather and frankincense really start to take hold. The result is a woody amber scent that feels lived in and worldly, like cracking open an old copy of The Portrait of Dorian Gray you found at the back of the library.

It’s sophisticated without being pretentious, and noticeable without being loud or overpowering. After applying two to three sprays to the neck and one to my wrists, the cologne maintains most of its strength for seven to eight hours, but lingers much longer. My girlfriend has noticed whiffs of it on my skin as much as 12 hours after applying it.

SHOP FULTON & ROARK CATHEDRAL OF RUINS EXTRAIT DE PARFUM

However, I am a fan of historic architecture, and that’s something Cathedral of Ruins nails. The combination of frankincense, saffron, and leather gives this fragrance a rustic, sophisticated feel reminiscent of a musky, old library or chic used bookstore in Upstate New York.

There is also, of course, a Gothic backdrop to the scent. I tend to get sad at the beginning of November, as it’s no longer socially acceptable to talk extensively about horror movies throughout the day now that Halloween is over. This cologne is ideal for those of us who know spooky season never ends.

It’s mysterious, alluring, and deep while still being approachable. It’s subtle enough for the office yet interesting enough for the bar. More than anything, it’s perfect for fall, capturing the beauty of falling leaves and cooling temperatures.

Cathedral of Ruins is for the guy who knows spooky season is a year-long commitment. Its woody amber profile blends cypress, pepper, rose, saffron, leather, and frankincense into a dark, sophisticated scent that feels equally at home in a library, bar, or office. It lasts for most of the day while staying noticeable without becoming overwhelming, making it an easy cold-weather go-to.

Make Your Scrambled Eggs Cracker Barrel-Style With This Easy Method (No Butter Required)

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

Cracker Barrel's best-selling menu item is a classic pancake, bacon, and egg combo, and no small part of that is because of the eggs. But according to a self-identified employee on Reddit, the old-timey establishment achieves such a delicious breakfast by keeping things simple, cooking them on a griddle with just canola oil — no butter.

When you include abundant fat in scrambled eggs, they slow down the coagulation of protein curds and keep them creamy and rich. By opting for a quick spray of canola oil instead, you can achieve a wildly different texture, allowing those proteins to join together for something a bit more solid and with a slight chew. Griddles also allow maximum surface area contact with direct heat, helping them cook quickly without burning them or making them tough. This is partially why breaking out the griddle is great when making breakfast for a crowd, and you can always add other foods like pancakes and bacon while you prepare the eggs.

Since you won't be able to rely on fat for texture, it's also important to use Cracker Barrel's preferred griddle temperature of 225 degrees Fahrenheit. This is closer to a medium heat, which keeps the eggs fluffy but still not quite creamy. The restaurant also employs one expert tip for quality scrambled eggs: using farm-fresh ingredients. These have a richer flavor to compensate for less fat and firmer whites to help keep the fluffy shape while you eat them.

When a breakfast item is so good but also so simple, a lot of its quality boils down to technique. Cooking with less fat gives you a smaller margin of error, and a griddle can take some getting used to, so keep your eggs in constant motion and don't be afraid to lean on a bit of added water to level up your breakfast.

When you cook scrambled eggs in a small pan, even a small amount will quickly drop its temperature. The same isn't necessarily true for a griddle, especially if you use one with a heavy-duty steel cooktop that retains a ton of heat, but it also responds quickly when the flame is lowered, allowing precise control of temperature throughout cooking. Using two spatulas to continuously move and fold your eggs is a great way to help them cook evenly. Plus, the successive folded layers only build on all that fluffiness and make them fantastic as an ingredient in things like a breakfast sandwich or burrito.

When it comes to keeping eggs fluffy, but not too rich or runny, your best bet is a simple splash of water. Added moisture, but no fat, steams the curds a bit, helping them plump up without growing dense. While too much will make them soupy and flavorless, a tablespoon per three eggs does a great job. The added moisture also helps prevent them from drying out when using something as hot and fast as a large surface area griddle, so it can be a great tool if you've never cooked on a flat-top before.

→ Baca artikel asli

United Wholesale Mortgage Pushes Through Q3 Amid Headwinds, Predicts Steady Q4

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

Despite having lower margins for the third quarter, United Wholesale Mortgage (UWM) Chairman, President and CEO Mat Ishbia stated that he feels prepared to handle “headwinds in the industry” during the company’s third quarter earnings call.

On the call, Ishbia referenced the challenges the mortgage market has faced within the past five years, saying that the market is “showing a lot of warts on a lot of different companies.”

“Everyone’s got some issue,” he stated. “A lot of other people are operating holes where they don’t have enough business. They don’t have enough opportunity to get loans, they can’t keep up production and they can’t invest in AI.”

Interest rates have weighed on the housing market heavily as of late, leaving mortgage lenders with lower demand. For UWM’s earnings however, Ishbia attributes the company’s success to the recent Oaktree partnership, which came together after an ugly bidding war with CrossCountry Mortgage.

“The best way to hedge is to do a lot more loans when rates drop, as long as you can stomach the Mortgage Servicing Right (MSR) values going down because rates drop,” Ishbia added. “We have a great hedging policy and process. We partnered with Oaktree on that and Vandy Fartaj. We feel really good about the process of how we’re hedging and how much to hedge, versus how much not to hedge.”

The latest Federal Reserve meeting in October concluded with no changes to rates, but mortgage rates have slowly begun to inch downward to the lower 7% range—around 7.24% according to Freddie Mac—after spiking dramatically from the September meeting’s rate hike.

“I think the natural hedge is the best way to do it, and we’re not back to the natural hedge yet,” he said.

Although the quarter’s loan origination volume came in at $33.4 billion—down from Q2 which was $39.7 billion, and the first quarter at $44.9 billion—Ishbia mentioned that expenses were down substantially, specifically tied into investments with AI technology.

“Those (expenses) are really just tied to the investments we’ve made in AI and technology over the last couple years when everyone thought our expenses were higher,” he noted. “And so we’re starting to see the fruits of our labor.”

In total, Q3 revenue clocked in around $811.1 million, which is down from last quarter’s $888 million and slightly down from last year’s revenue of around $843 million.

Adjusted EBITDA came in at $178.3 million, less than last quarter and last year’s estimates ($185.9 million and $211.1 million, respectively).

UWM’s stock grew following the earnings call, as the earnings report beat out expectations. The company’s stock jumped nearly 13%, but is still down about 59% year to date.

Net loss was down quarterly in Q3, to $212.6 million compared to Q2 at $451.9 million. It was significantly higher than last year’s Q3 net loss however ($12.1 million). As an improvement from the previous quarter, Ishbia again credits the lower expenses to the company’s AI investments now panning out.

“AI can definitely help you cut costs and expenses, and you’re seeing some of that benefit, but you have to make a massive investment in it to do that. As you can see, it helps you do more loans, grow your business and drive the top line revenue,” he commented.

As for market share, Ishbia emphasizes the power and role brokers play in UWM’s success. He described brokers as the “growing channel” and states that they are the “winners right now.”

“UWM’s market share is fantastic. We’re strong, we’re dominant, and we’re going to continue to grow. If we lose market share, I hope that happens because that means the broker market has gotten so big and it’s going to keep growing, that’s my focus,” he continued. “My focus is growing the broker channel, helping every loan officer do a little bit more loans, helping every real estate agent that works with brokers do more business, and helping them all win.”

With 2026 winding down, Ishbia sees the upcoming year as a time of “optimization” but said he expected the mortgage market to remain tough for some time. He also notes that UWM focuses primarily on purchase mortgages, and acknowledges the challenges that refinance mortgage loans could face with current rates.

“Since we’re mostly purchase, I’m not as concerned about the volume going down as much as a lot of refinance shops will,” he concluded. “Traditionally, the fourth quarter and first quarter are always the tougher times in the mortgage market. But once again, expenses are down, AI is winning and brokers are growing. We feel like we’re in the best position of anybody to capitalize on opportunity.”

Prior to joining RISMedia, Desirae wrote stories geared toward policy issues for the Connecticut Mirror. She also worked as a staff writer for The Daily Campus, UConn's student-run newspaper.

Hearts accounts reveal £2.4m loss and transfer spend amid bad news on Calvin Miller injury

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

Hearts have announced a loss of £2.4million after investing heavily to return to European football this season.

The Tynecastle club backed Derek McInnes after he took over as boss in the summer of 2025. The Jambos’ thrilling Scottish Premiership title challenge went to the last day of the season when they lost to Celtic at Parkhead to finish second to the Hoops.

Did you know with a Digital Subscription to The Scotsman, you can get unlimited access to the website including our premium content, as well as benefiting from fewer ads, loyalty rewards and much more.

Hearts lost to Sturm Graz in the Champions League qualifiers and then to Benfica in the Europa League, but victory over Rapid Vienna saw Wouter Vrancken’s side make it to the league phase of the Conference League, where they face Swiss side Thun on Thursday.

Hearts also reported a turnover of £23.1m for the 2025/26 financial year, are also in “positive” discussions about further investment to help Vrancken in the January transfer window and are confident of a “significantly improved financial result” in a year’s time.

The club’s financial results also revealed that staff costs went up eight per cent “with player acquisition costs of £7.8m across the year, reflecting significant investment in the playing squad”.

A statement on Hearts’ official website said: “The club took a deliberate decision to invest in the playing squad from a position of financial strength, helping secure a return to European league phase football for 2026/27. This additional investment in squad wages, combined with the absence of European income, resulted in an operating loss of £2.4m for the year.

“With European income returning and further progress in player trading, the club anticipates a significantly improved financial result in 2026/27.

“Strong underlying revenue growth offset much of the loss of European income for the 2025/26 financial year, with sponsorship and advertising income up 37 per cent and retail and hospitality income up 17 per cent, reflecting continued commercial progress and support from partners and fans alike.

“The club continued to benefit from exceptional financial backing from its benefactors and Foundation of Hearts members, and extends its sincere thanks for their continued commitment and support.

“The increased investment in the playing squad and football operations was supported by an investment from Tony Bloom towards the end of the previous financial year, which strengthened the club’s financial position and provided capacity to invest in improving the playing squad.

“As an equity investment, this funding is reflected in the balance sheet rather than recognised as income in the profit and loss account.

“The reported operating loss of £2.4m includes £1.8m of gains from player trading and is stated before depreciation, amortisation and finance costs. Further gains will be reflected in the 2026/27 financial year following player trading activity completed in July and August 2026.

“The club is also engaged in positive discussions regarding further investment to support the continued, measured pursuit of the club’s ambitions. The club’s balance sheet remains robust, with the accounts reporting net assets of £20.3m at the June 30 2026 financial year-end.”

Meanwhile, Vrancken revealed Calvin Miller will require surgery on a knee injury that will keep him out for two months.

Both Tomas Magnusson and Miller came off during the first half of the Jambos’ 2-1 Premier Sports Cup semi-final defeat to Edinburgh rivals Hibs at Hampden on Sunday. The Iceland midfielder was taken to hospital with concussion and the former Celtic, Morton and Falkirk attacker left the national stadium on crutches and his knee in a brace.

Vrancken, who will be without 10 first-team players for the game against FC Thun, was asked about Miller and said: “It will be two months, let’s say, depending on what the surgery says because it’s a meniscus injury. It’s a pity, but it is what it is.

“Tomas is doing good. The day after was already a lot better. There is a protocol, of course, with concussions. He will be for sure back after the (international) break.”

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

Fitness influencer Goob’s apparent arrest sent social media into a frenzy after Joey Swoll posted footage of him being taken into custody, but Suffolk County police now say the entire scene was staged to promote a charity food giveaway.

Goob, whose real name is John Dorsey, is known for creating fitness content and elaborate holiday charity promotions. His latest Instagram reel told viewers to “Please watch until the end” while tagging the Suffolk County Police Department, HummusFit and GymKatie.

Joey Swoll, another prominent fitness influencer, initially presented the footage as a serious incident. His original Instagram post showed Dorsey apparently being escorted by police and was captioned, “Welcome to the show John.”

“I’m not gonna say what it is that he did, because I don’t have all the facts yet,” Swoll said. He then told his friend to “do better.”

Goob later addressed the footage on Instagram, asking followers for “privacy and grace” while he dealt with the situation. He also shared what appeared to be a letter from an attorney warning him about possible criminal charges and advising him to remain silent.

Despite the growing speculation, Dorsey insisted, “It’s not a skit.”

The story changed after a Suffolk County Police Department spokesperson told TMZ that Dorsey was never actually arrested.

“It was part of filming social media posts advertising a charity food giveaway that is taking place later this month,” the spokesperson said.

Swoll later posted a follow-up reel captioned “UPDATE! 😳😳😳 @goob_u2,” while Goob’s own follow-up video asked viewers to watch until the end.

Bitcoin rebounds past $80,000 as markets shrug off midterms and Binance hack

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

Get The Block’s latest crypto news and price alerts for the coins you follow, right in Chrome. Add our free extension here.

Ether jumped nearly 6% and crypto stocks rallied, while Bitwise says the market’s muted reaction to the Binance hack shows growing maturity.

Bitcoin has recovered from its post-Binance hack losses, surging toward $81,000 following Tuesday's U.S. midterm elections, which saw Democrats regain control of both chambers of Congress.

"Markets took the Democratic win in stride," Nexo analysts wrote in their daily dispatch. "Futures fell as much as 2.5% on early counts and then recovered fully, which suggests investors saw the outcome as adding clarity and not as changing the growth story."

Bitcoin (BTC) was up 3.5% to $80,500 on Wednesday afternoon, while Ethereum jumped nearly 6% to $2,433. Solana and XRP were up a more muted 2.5% on the day.

Futures data suggests traders are adjusting existing positions rather than taking on additional leverage, according to Nexo. Crypto futures volume climbed 27%, while open interest rose just 3%, and Bitcoin open interest grew more slowly than its price. Roughly $469 million in leveraged positions were also flushed out.

Nexo said market orders have shifted to a slight net buying bias after favoring sellers the previous day, while the most actively traded Bitcoin options are predominantly calls.

Crypto-related stocks also rallied, with Strategy and Coinbase gaining about 6% each; Strategy's variable-rate perpetual preferred stock STRC hit $100 for the first time since May.

"The case for holding Bitcoin crosses party lines," Executive Chairman Michael Saylor said earlier Wednesday.

Block Inc. jumped 15% following its earnings report, while bitcoin treasury firm Strive climbed roughly 12%. Meanwhile, the Official Trump (TRUMP) memecoin fell 17%.

Bitwise CIO Matt Hougan argued that the market's relatively muted reaction to the Binance hack reflects the crypto industry's growing maturity and improved security infrastructure.

In his weekly blog memo, Hougan examined the 10 largest crypto-related hacks in history and found that Bitcoin finished higher in the following 30 days in five cases and lower in five, suggesting no consistent relationship between major security incidents and Bitcoin's subsequent performance.

He said Bitcoin's roughly 4% decline following the Binance breach was largely in line with broader risk assets, emphasizing that Binance was hacked, not the Bitcoin network.

"Hacks would impact the market if they suggested there is no safer way to hold bitcoin, but there is. In this case, the stolen crypto was held in online hot wallets at a large, integrated crypto exchange," Hougan wrote.

He pointed to institutional custodians, cold storage and spot Bitcoin ETFs as increasingly secure alternatives for investors, highlighting ETFs in particular because their underlying assets are held in offline cold storage and subject to multiple withdrawal approvals and restrictions.

Hougan's assessment was echoed by Matt Mena, crypto research strategist at 21Shares, who said the market's resilience following the Binance breach was encouraging.

"What we find most encouraging is how the market handled the worst of it," Mena wrote in a Tuesday report. "The largest hack in crypto’s history caused a far smaller drawdown than past crises; the protocols kept running, and none of the structural drivers broke: banks, payment networks, and asset managers kept shipping onchain products through the drawdown, and regulators kept writing rules."

Mena is watching four things into November: whether Bitcoin holds $76,000 to $78,000 and sustains its move back above $80,000 to $82,000; Binance's progress in completing withdrawals and proving its reserves; credit spreads, oil prices, and long-end Treasury yields; and whether ETF inflows return.

Google Is Forcing AI Overviews in Classrooms. Schools Are Searching for a Way Out

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

Two weeks ago, an IT administrator for an elementary-middle school took to Google’s support forums requesting a way to block students from seeing AI Overviews—the chatbot-generated answers at the top of search results.

“This seems counterproductive to what we're trying to teach,” the administrator wrote. “Students need opportunities to navigate search results, examine sources, and draw their own conclusions rather than being handed an AI-generated synthesis before they've even started.”

No one from Google responded to the concern, which has been bubbling up for years. But counterparts from three districts voiced similar frustration and pointed to work-arounds they were employing or considering to block AI Overviews in lieu of an official “off” switch. Their expertise is about to be in demand.

Google announced on Tuesday that it is declining to provide schools an option to disable AI Overviews. Instead, starting next year, schools will have to block access to search altogether to get rid of the feature. The decision has stunned educators, parents, and technology researchers across the US.

“We were flabbergasted,” says Dominique Johnson, part of an Oregon community group called Lake Oswego Parents for Responsible Technology. “This move makes things even more difficult for school districts that have determined AI is harmful to foundational learning.”

Google didn’t respond to a request for comment. But in a public response to the controversy shared on X, the company contended that schools will now have “more choice, not less” because search, its Gemini chatbot, and Gemini Notebook tool will be off by default starting next year. Administrators can enable one, two, or all three services.

The issue for schools and parents, though, is that Google considers AI Overviews and a companion feature called AI Mode inseparable from the traditional search experience. Respectively launched in 2023 and 2025, they bring the functionality of AI chatbots, such as Gemini or ChatGPT, directly into search results.

“Kids are already using these search-bar summaries to skip real effort, pulling instant math answers and letting AI write their essays,” Johnson says. “To make matters worse, these AI snapshots frequently make mistakes and output false facts, which younger students take as absolute truth.”

The AI-generated responses are tempting to read and are often good enough to trust, triggering headaches beyond classrooms. Wikipedia, news websites, and education tools have lost human visitors as people rely on overviews. Google uses the same bots to crawl the web for its search and AI products, making it impossible for publishers to opt out of their content being used for the latter.

Google’s push to make AI Overviews a prominent part of search has already come at a cost. Last week, shares of parent company Alphabet suffered their worst single-day selloff, erasing half a trillion dollars in market value after the company reported weaker-than-expected search ad sales. A big cause? Increased usage of AI Overviews and AI Mode, which aren’t packed with many ads yet—although some experts believe the slump will only be temporary.

For schools, the dilemma over search may be just beginning. While several districts WIRED contacted didn’t respond to requests for comment, Los Angeles Unified says it is still reviewing the potential implications of Google’s announcement. Some education technology experts argue that schools should abandon Google search altogether, or at least do a better job teaching students to question the validity of AI answers.

“Under-resourced districts will grit their teeth and say, ‘We don’t have the capacity to dig in our heels and not use Google,’” says Stephen Aguilar, who coleads the Center for Generative AI and Society at USC. “A lot are likely going to accelerate plans they already have to teach students about this new digital literacy.”

Parent advocates also plan to push districts to look elsewhere. “This move just continues a well-established pattern of big tech companies pushing products into classrooms,” says Karolle Rabarison, a policy and communications strategist who helps lead Schools Beyond Screens in Washington, DC. “It’s not going to work anymore.”

Tech industry veterans disagree about how difficult it would be for Google to let schools disable AI Overviews without affecting the rest of Search. “It’s a question of whether they’re committed to maintaining two versions of search in the long run,” says Nicole Kerson, a former senior Google Search product manager who runs the AI strategy firm Collected Company. “It's very hard to maintain a forked product like that.”

Microsoft offers a Google Chrome extension that lets users turn off AI-generated summaries in Bing search. Adeel Khan, a former teacher and principal who now runs MagicSchool, which provides AI chatbots for students and teachers, says it is “infuriating, insulting, and ludicrous” that Google isn’t doing something similar.

Without an official way to disable AI Overviews, schools have turned to technical work-arounds, including modifying search URLs and adopting browser extensions that change how Google results appear. Fairfax County Public Schools in Virginia recently developed its own extension. But these loopholes could stop functioning if Google changes how its search results are delivered.

Smaller search engines, such as the privacy-focused DuckDuckGo, see an opportunity to win business from schools frustrated with Google. “I wouldn't expect to see a Google-style DuckDuckGo Classroom anytime soon, but ideally, we'll have special resources just for educators looking to make a switch,” says spokesperson Allison Goodman.

Following a federal ruling in 2024 that Google illegally maintained its search monopoly, the company was ordered to share certain search data and services with rivals and loosen restrictions on how its products are distributed. But the effort is well behind schedule, thanks to ongoing legal disputes slowing the independent committee meant to oversee the remedies. The AI Overviews issue could prompt fresh scrutiny as several lawmakers, including US senator Bernie Sanders, criticized Google’s stance toward schools.

Studies examining the effects of the new AI tools entering schools have raised concerns about whether they are eroding opportunities for students to develop skills such as critical thinking, according to a Stanford review of the research as of a year ago. According to a survey conducted by Google, three in four teenagers report using AI weekly, but only about half say they check its outputs for accuracy. A recent study by Washington University in St. Louis, meanwhile, found that only about 42 percent of AI Overviews could be fully verified using the citations they contained. Google has said overviews undergo rigorous testing and are continually improving.

In its response to the recent furor over AI Overviews, Google said it plans to launch a new feature for students in the coming months called Guided Learning. The idea is to coach users to find information rather than provide them with an immediate answer. “We believe AI products should support ‘productive struggle’—enabling students to be actively challenged without getting so overwhelmed that they give up,” wrote Chris Phillips, Google’s vice president for education, in the company’s blog post on Tuesday.

Some researchers are already questioning how effective Guided Learning will be, arguing that it won’t be as natural as working with a human tutor. Aguilar views Guided Learning as another attempt by Google to maintain the position it already occupies in American classrooms, where Google-powered Chromebook laptops, Google Classroom learning software, Search, and YouTube are commonplace.

“There’s a kernel of truth in saying we have to prepare students for a world where AI is ubiquitous,” Aguilar says, “but it shouldn’t be through a company making a very self-serving pronouncement because it’s heavily invested in AI.”

Pro wrestler who plays evil politician elected mayor of Providence, Rhode Island

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

A professional wrestler who portrays a corrupt politician in the ring has been elected mayor of Providence, Rhode Island, proving that sometimes the bad guy really does win.

David Morales, a 28-year-old Democrat and state representative, defeated Republican Dave Talan in the November 3 election, securing approximately 81% of the vote compared to his opponent’s 17%.

According to Rhode Island Current, Morales will become the youngest mayor in Providence history when he takes office in January 2027.

However, his political career isn’t the only thing that makes him stand out. Morales also happens to be a three-time wrestling champion who plays an evil politician.

Independent professional wrestler David Morales was elected mayor of Providence, Rhode Island in last night’s midterm elections pic.twitter.com/vFjAIABtlQ

Since 2021, Morales has competed in Rhode Island’s Renegade Wrestling Alliance under the name “The Commissioner,” portraying an arrogant political authority figure who loves getting under the crowd’s skin.

He’s even won the promotion’s Hype Championship three times, and once introduced a fictional rule requiring the champion to carry their title belt everywhere, including the statehouse.

In an October 2025 interview with Ocean State Media, Morales discussed the similarities between his two careers.

“I think there’s a lot of overlap,” he said. “Including showmanship, theater, athleticism, drama and humor.”

Ironically, Morales believes politicians have actually started taking too much inspiration from professional wrestling, warning that copying the exaggerated promos of WWE stars can lead to extremist rhetoric.

His wrestling career even became a talking point during the election campaign.

According to The Boston Globe, political opponents distributed campaign materials featuring Morales in his wrestling attire, accusing him of being “all hype, no results.”

Man wins election after telling voters he’s “unqualified” and has no idea what he’s doing

Seth Rollins’ next fight: Logan Paul, Roman Reigns among options for former champion

WWE Legend Kane reignites Die Hard Christmas movie debate and he’s not backing down

Clearly, voters weren’t particularly bothered by his extracurricular activities.

Morales announced his semi-retirement from wrestling while pursuing the mayor’s office, but hasn’t ruled out stepping back into the ring.

“Never say never in the world of professional wrestling,” he said.

Of course, Morales isn’t the first wrestler to trade the squared circle for public office.

WWE legend Kane, whose real name is Glenn Jacobs, was elected mayor of Knox County, Tennessee, which includes Knoxville, in 2018. He served until 2026 and even found time to reignite the debate over whether Die Hard is a Christmas movie while in office.

Former WWE star Jesse Ventura also famously became governor of Minnesota in 1999.

With Morales now heading to City Hall, Providence residents will have to wait and see whether The Commissioner makes an appearance during his time in office.

Xbox is testing two new controller options for Cloud Gaming

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

The Nano Controller is adopting a Google Stadia trick.

Just ahead of new Xbox Cloud Gaming time limits kicking in this month, Microsoft is letting folks try two new controller options for the service. If you stream Xbox games on a supported smart TV, you'll be able to use your phone as a controller. The interface opens in a browser, so you won't need to download a dedicated app. Some games have custom controller layouts, Microsoft said.

The company is making this option available today on select TVs. To try it, go to Settings > Account in the Cloud Gaming interface on your TV and enable Preview Features.

Other companies with game-streaming services let you use your phone as a controller, including Netflix. It's maybe not the best option for games that are fast paced or have complex controls, but for the likes of turn-based titles, your phone could be a useful way to play if you don't have a controller nearby.

In addition, folks in the US can connect the new Nano Controller directly to Xbox Cloud Gaming via Wi-Fi. The idea is to help reduce latency.

After you set up the gamepad using the Xbox beta app on Android or Xbox Accessories app on PC, you can follow directions to connect your Nano Controller to Wi-Fi. (Note that if you're going through the PC app, you'll need to be enrolled in the Xbox Insider testing program to try out Wi-Fi connectivity.) Then, when you access Xbox Cloud Gaming through the same account you used to set up the controller, the gamepad will connect via Wi-Fi.

This is a smart way to try to reduce input lag, but again, it's not the first time we've seen a controller that can connect to Wi-Fi. The gamepad for Google's defunct Stadia cloud gaming service was able to do that too. (Stadia let players use phones as controllers for TV use as well.)

Microsoft said both of these controller options are in preview. It noted that availability, and the supported devices and games, may be limited for now. For instance, Nano Controller Wi-Fi connectivity only works on smart TVs and the web for the time being.

ER Pediatrician Shares Her Top 8 Cold & Flu Products for Kids

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

Not much is certain about parenting, except that kids get sick… and it happens a lot. Just as they get over the sniffles, they come home with a stomach bug, pick up an ear infection, and maybe another cold because why not? To make matters worse, this cycle seems to repeat itself throughout the entire winter season. But never fear! Our favorite TikTok doc, Dr. Meghan Martin, a pediatric emergency medicine doctor and mom of four, recently shared her top cold and flu medicines and products to keep kids (somewhat) comfortable while they’re down for the count.

Related: Pediatric Nurse Shares 3 Signs That Your Sick Kid Needs to Go to the ER

Yep. Make sure you always have some of these when your kids are sick. Or even better, carry them with you everywhere. Martin likes these because they’re disposable, twist-and-tie, and can be easily disposed of in a trash can. Plus, they’re designed to fit your kiddo’s mouth perfectly, which means less chance of the vomit making its way somewhere else—like your car or their bed.

These cold and flu medicines for kids are staples for any parent. You can rotate between the two so kids don’t ever have to feel uncomfortable, and Martin explains that there are different ways to offer them depending on your child’s preferences. “You have tubules, you have liquids, and you can also get the acetaminophen suppositories if they have hand, foot, and mouth or are just refusing meds,” she says.

While the no-touch forehead thermometers have been all the rage, Dr. Martin advises against using them regularly. “They’re expensive, and they’re not accurate,” she says. Her preferred model is the classic digital thermometer. Most of them can take temperatures orally, under the arm, or rectally, and produce accurate temperature readings within a few seconds.

According to Martin, these devices work well on medium- and normal-sized hands, and while they aren’t as great on tiny hands, the reading will still give you an idea of their heart rate and how oxygen levels are doing. An important note: Martin mentioned that if you have a darker skin tone, it doesn’t work as well when it gets to the lower numbers. “If you are concerned, ignore this, and just take your kiddo in,” she says.

Instead of Benadryl, Martin recommends keeping Zyrtec on hand for allergic reactions or hives. In fact, the FDA warns against using antihistamines in children ages two or younger and recommends use only for kids ages six and up.

If there’s one thing parents know to keep on hand during cold and flu season, it’s rehydrating Pedialyte. But instead of the liquid version, these powdered packs can be added to a water bottle. “They fit in the box better than a big jug of the liquid. Kids need to replace sugar and electrolytes when they’re sick and not eating regular food, and this is a balanced way to do that,” says Martin.

Martin doesn’t recommend decongestants for kids and is a huge fan of saline spray. She suggests giving older kids the nose mist/spray or buying packets to use in a squeeze bottle to help irrigate their clogged passages, and she emphasizes that parents should not use tap water in these mixtures in any circumstances. For young kids, Martin recommends using a saline mist inhaler, and for babies, nose drops and the Friday Baby snotsucker or a suction bulb are your best bet.

Martin does like to have a kid-friendly version of cough drops on hand to help soothe bad coughs and sore throats, and the brilliant part is that they’re lollipops.

If you’ve got your cold & flu box at the ready, you’re never caught off guard when sickness strikes. Good luck, parents!

Bringing a little more joy to parenthood.

For small-business owners who love to fly with American Airlines, the (see rates and fees) should be in your crosshairs.

The is a mid-tier airline card with standout perks like free checked bags, no-additional-fee employee cards and Loyalty Points on spending.

Given that checking bags with American costs at least $45 per bag one-way, you can practically recoup this card's annual fee after the first cardholder year by checking a bag on three one-way flights. If you travel with an employee or another companion, it becomes easy to extract even more value — assuming you would otherwise pay for checked bags.

You'll need to spend $30,000 in a cardholder year to earn a companion certificate ($99 plus taxes and fees). That's an incredibly high spending requirement for a mid-tier card that earns just 1 mile per dollar on nonbonus purchases.

The card includes basic business management resources, but its benefits still lean heavily toward American Airlines travel rather than premium business tools.

You won’t get statement credits for business partners or other features offered by more robust business cards. From a perks standpoint, the Citi / AAdvantage Business is extremely similar to the personal , though the earning rates are better suited to business spending.

Cardholders could also get a lot of value out of the choice category once it launches in 2027. When it's time to redeem the miles you earn, your best bet is to book award flights. In addition to flights operated by American Airlines, you can use your miles on partner airlines such as Alaska Airlines or Finnair.

The Citi / AAdvantage Business will become more useful after its 2027 refresh, but its annual fee is rising from $99 to $150. That makes it harder to justify if you don’t use the new inflight or Global Entry or TSA PreCheck credits.

Most purchases will still earn just 1 mile per dollar, and only one of the three selectable business categories can earn 2 miles per dollar at a time.

Related: Which AAdvantage card is right for you? Here are 3 options you should consider

The AAdvantage Business and Platinum Select share most of their airline perks, so the decision comes down to whether you need a business account, which bonus categories fit your spending and whether you value the Business card’s companion certificate or the Platinum Select’s new lifestyle perks.

The Platinum Select, though, has stronger rewards on restaurant spending and will add bonus spending categories for select streaming services, electric vehicle charging and eligible rides and rails purchases beginning Jan. 24, 2027.

You'll still get free checked bags, preferred boarding, Loyalty Points on card spending and inflight savings with the Platinum Select.

Choose the Citi / AAdvantage Business if you want to separate your business expenses, add employee cards and earn bonus miles on more business-specific purchases, like telecommunications, U.S. advertising, U.S. shipping or office supplies.

The Platinum Select is the better fit if you don’t need a business account. Because their core American Airlines benefits overlap, most travelers won’t need both.

The Citi / AAdvantage Business is worth it for small-business owners who fly American often enough to use the airline benefits. The addition of statement credits toward eligible inflight purchases could provide more value to offset the annual fee, even though that cost is going up in 2027.

At $150 after the first year, it will be less compelling for cardholders who already receive similar American Airlines perks from another card or via elite status. It’s also not ideal as an everyday spending card, since most purchases earn just 1 mile per dollar spent.

For a limited time, new cardholders can earn 75,000 bonus miles after spending $5,000 on purchases in the first five months from account opening. This offer is the highest we've seen in the card’s recent public offers, making it an excellent time to apply.

The Motorola Signature 27 Has a Dedicated Action Sensor and Ludicrously Bright Screen

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

The Motorola Signature 27 Has a Dedicated Action Sensor and Ludicrously Bright Screen

Motorola debuted its brand new flagship phone, the Signature 27, at Qualcomm’s annual Snapdragon Summit in September. However, at the time, Motorola unveiled just a few key specs and left much to the imagination. That changed this week, with the company revealing more information about its new phone.

As PetaPixel previously reported, the sleek Signature 27’s main camera is the 50-megapixel Sony Lytia 910, a relatively large Type 1/1.28 LOFIC image sensor. It promises excellent low-light performance, expanded dynamic range, and gorgeous color.

It’s paired with a 200-megapixel Sony Lytia 901 image sensor for the telephoto camera. This is an even bigger sensor, measuring at Type 1/1.12. The periscope telephoto camera has 5x optical zoom and, through AI image processing, can reach 100x equivalent zoom with Super Zoom Pro. As Motorola notes, this uses generative AI to create new pixels. This is similar to other super zoom features seen on competing Android smartphones.

It also has a third standard rear camera: a 50-megapixel ultrawide with a Type 1/2.76 sensor.

However, in an exclusive hands-on video with Motorola, YouTuber creator Danny Winget revealed more details about the Signature 27, including a special sensor on the rear camera module.

Describing the camera system as “insane,” Winget explains that a dedicated “action sensor” on the back of the Signature 27 helps with high-speed photography. It’s not entirely clear what this sensor actually is, but as Winget shows, it looks effective with the camera’s dedicated “action mode.” It’s worth keeping an eye on.

Winget also discussed the Signature 27’s large 6.8-inch display, which is a Tandem OLED that Motorola claims tops out at 12,000 nits, which is absurd. The Samsung Galaxy S26 Ultra doesn’t even hit 3,000. While 12,000-nit peak brightness will likely be limited to specific situations and only part of the screen, it is still a pretty wild spec.

Tandem OLED display tech recently made news elsewhere in the tech space, as Apple’s brand-new, redesigned touchscreen MacBook Pro also features a new Tandem OLED.

As PetaPixel noted in September, the Signature 27 runs on Qualcomm’s newest Snapdragon 8 Elite chip, features a refined, fashion-forward design, and includes Bang & Olufsen audio.

Motorola is definitely targeting a premium market here, although it’s not known yet just how premium. It’s also still unclear exactly when the Motorola Signature 27 will launch. The company is slow-rolling the reveal. It is expected to fully launch sometime this month, though, so prospective buyers shouldn’t have to wait much longer for all the details.

Businesses Treat Your AI Agent 3 Different Ways via @sejournal, @slobodanmanic

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

This webinar uses Conductor's research to help you prioritize marketing data and AI visibility measurement fixes.

This rundown shows how to test briefs, audits, or rank reporting with a four-layer system built on your own data.

This webinar uses Conductor's research to help you prioritize marketing data and AI visibility measurement fixes.

This rundown shows how to test briefs, audits, or rank reporting with a four-layer system built on your own data.

Ask the SEJ experts you already read what AI, agents, and algorithm changes mean for your traffic. Private, human to human. $97/mo.

An AI agent does things its customer would not, on that customer’s behalf. Three businesses answered that problem three ways in October.

People are starting to send AI agents to do things for them online: Buy the groceries, book the flight, check the bank balance. The agent goes to the business’s website and acts in the customer’s place. Every business now has to decide what to do when one shows up, and in October three big ones answered three different ways.

I think Walmart got it right: It is about to give each agent a separate profile inside the customer’s account. An AI agent will do things the person maybe wouldn’t have, so the two are not the same. But it does those things on the person’s behalf, so they need to be linked. A separate profile lets the business see both: the agent and the customer who sent it.

Walmart says why it built Agent Profiles: “Today, when a customer uses an AI agent, it signs in using a customer’s account. This means that all purchases – whether made by the customer or the agent – appear together, without any differentiation.”

An agent might browse more pages than the customer would, maybe all of them. That’s the most basic difference. Beyond that, it could be literally anything.

So it’s not as simple as letting the agent sign in as the customer. Send a dog to shop for you at a local market, with a set amount of money. The dog only does it to bring the shopping back to you, and the whole thing only makes sense if the sellers know who sent the dog.

Walmart’s spending limit is the set amount of money, and the profile is the sellers knowing who sent it. A block stops the customer from using an agent at all. A shared login hides the agent behind the customer.

DoorDash says outside AI agents have placed “maybe several hundred orders in total”, in CEO Tony Xu’s words on the Q3 call, as TechCrunch reported. In the same quarter DoorDash handled 993 million orders, more than 10 million a day.

The rules for how an agent tells a business who it is are still a draft. The AI customer-service company Sierra and Meta are writing them as the Personal Agent Protocol: 40-plus companies are helping shape it, and its website named no business using it with customers when I checked on 1 November. On October 30, the draft added a recommendation that agents sign their requests with Web Bot Auth, a proposed way for an agent to prove which company runs it. According to the bot-protection company DataDome, as PPC Land reported, Meta’s Muse agent has been signing some of its requests that way since around 12 October, without an announcement.

Do three things before agents arrive in numbers:

If you can’t tell the agent from the customer, you can’t treat it differently.

Stablecoin adoption hits new highs: Tron reaches 76.53M holders, RLUSD climbs past $2.5B

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

Stablecoin adoption continues to grow on various blockchain platforms. Against that growth, TRON appears to be the most widely held platform by the number of stablecoin holders.

In Q3 2026, TRON had an average of 76.53 million stablecoin holders. Therefore, it accounts for approximately 40.45% of all the stablecoin holders on the 45 blockchain platforms that were monitored.

These figures indicate that TRON [TRX] was significantly ahead of Ethereum [ETH]  with an average of 26.32 million stablecoin holders.

These results reflect the increasing use of stablecoins as a medium of exchange for making transfers and payments and a reflection of the increased demand to move around digital money.

Also, the 30-day average of TRON has been above 79 million in Q4, indicating continued support for the platform. This growing user base could attract more stablecoin activity, deepen network liquidity, and reinforce TRON’s position in digital payments.

While TRON leads stablecoin adoption by holder numbers, Ripple USD [RLUSD] is gaining ground through rising supply and inflows. Notably, the current circulation supply for RLUSD has reached over $2.5 billion.

Although this rapid price increase represented a gain of $150 million for RLUSD, it does not appear that this represents new investor demand. Instead, it resulted from Ripple’s massive minting effort.

RLUSD still trails its historical high of $2.632 billion, so there exists potential for future growth. Continued inflows and increased use of the asset will ultimately determine if this increase in circulation supply results in additional long-term adoption and competitive advantage in the market.

The rise in stablecoin holders and RLUSD supply now raises a bigger question: are users actively moving these assets across networks? While TRON shows actual use of these assets, it appears that Tether [USDT] accounts nearly 98% of TRON’s stablecoin supply, standing at $94.3 billion.

This concentration supports heavy payment and transfer activity, reflected in 3.9 million daily active addresses and over 12 million transactions. The network also generates roughly $1.1 million in daily fees, showing the economic value of this activity.

TRON [TRX] leads stablecoin adoption, with 76.53 million holders and strong USDT activity.

Ripple USD [RLUSD] has surpassed $2.5 billion in supply, but XRPL usage remains weaker.

Opinion: Liverpool's next Steven Gerrard announces himself, what an assist

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

What an assist that was. Liverpool's next Steven Gerrard continues to go from strength to strength.

Yes, the 'next Steven Gerrard' label is overdone when it comes to talking about Luca Eden. The teenager is taking Liverpool's youth system by storm, performing wonders every time we see him.

It's enough that Eden has been promoted full-on into the Under 19s now. He's a UEFA Youth League regular and the youngest goalscorer Liverpool have ever had in the competition at just 15.

But it's important to take note of what 'next Gerrard' actually means. No one believes Eden is going to have anywhere near the impact on the first team that Gerrard had, nor is there any expectation for him to do so.

This club is well over 125 years old and Gerrard still looks like a one-of-a-kind player. Eden will be his own player and carve his own path in the game and it's virtually impossible to know exactly how good he'll go on to be.

What we mean is that stylistically Eden is the closest we've seen to Gerrard. And he keeps proving that point.

Luca Eden keeps doing itIt wasn't a goal for Eden on Wednesday but we did see an assist. And what an assist. The teenager burst through midfield with the ball, picking up on some uncertainty from three separate players around him. That's the kind of moment that brings Gerrard comparisons, in temperament as much as anything. Eden took the game by the scruff of its neck, even just for a moment. He left two opponents in his dust before playing a perfect through-ball for Will Wright to score his hattrick goal.

It wasn't a goal for Eden on Wednesday but we did see an assist. And what an assist.

Eden took the game by the scruff of its neck, even just for a moment. He left two opponents in his dust before playing a perfect through-ball for Will Wright to score his hattrick goal.

It was fantastic all-around. Wright deserves the most plaudits from this match, unquestionably, given he scored three ans assisted another.

Eden, though, must not fall under the radar. He's doing things that take you far in the game, demonstrating instinct and drive that establish you in teams. He also has a supreme confidence despite being so young. Again, that takes you far.

So while expectation can't enter the equation, it's impossible not to be excited by Eden. Every time he's out there, he looks more like a real player and it does feel like the sky's the limit here.
