New Dunkin' Donuts Commercial Explains The Biggest Threat The Avengers Face In Doomsday

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

Marvel Cinematic Universe die-hards might have a more complete understanding of what awaits in "Avengers: Doomsday," but the average viewer just knows the Avengers are going up against Robert Downey Jr.'s Victor Von Doom. Now, however, a wise sage has arrived to explain the plot of the upcoming superhero team up: Dunkin' Donuts. A new commercial for the company's Marvel Studios collab, available on YouTube, sees Channing Tatum's Gambit laying out the premise of "Doomsday" with the help of the Fantastic Four's H.E.R.B.I.E. in the most straightforward way possible.

"Doomsday" takes place fourteen months after the events of "Thunderbolts*," and sees three groups of heroes team up to take on Doctor Doom. Just what Doom is up to in the movie remains somewhat unclear at this point. Thus far, the "Doomsday" trailers have mainly focused on hyping up Doom's arrival and the return of beloved characters from MCU history. We do know the Avengers, New Avengers, Wakandans, Fantastic Four, and the 20th Century Fox-era X-Men will be putting up a united front against RDJ's villain, but otherwise we're eagerly awaiting the film's December 18, 2026 release to find out just how he fits into the story.

In the meantime, Channing's Gambit and H.E.R.B.I.E. (Humanoid Experimental Robot, B-Type, Integrated Electronics) are here to offer a simplified explanation of the existential threat facing the MCU. Marvel has teamed up with Dunkin' to deliver a new range of treats that look at once delicious and capable of dissolving your insides in a matter of seconds. Turns out, these new menu items are also pretty handy for explaining the impending collapse of the multiverse.

In the Dunkin' promo clip, entitled "Incoming Munchkins Incursion!" H.E.R.B.I.E. and Gambit are seen in Avengers Tower. The robot points out the window at a planet seemingly on a collision course with the one on which he and Gambit sit. He then uses two donut hole Munchkin treats to represent worlds colliding with one another, in what's known as an incursion, thus explaining the core problem at the heart of "Avengers: Doomsday" more clearly and succinctly than any of the trailers so far.

We first learned about incursions in the sort of fun Sam Raimi movie "Doctor Strange in the Multiverse of Madness," wherein John Krasinski's Reed Richards explained how "an incursion occurs when the boundary between two universes erodes, destroying one or both entirely." In "Doomsday," it seems multiple incursions are happening at the same time, threatening the existence of the entire multiverse. As various universes collide with one another, characters from different timelines (i.e. different eras of Marvel's cinematic history) come into contact, hence why "Doomsday" features characters from so many different films. We sort of knew "Doomsday" would feature multiple incursions, given the events of films leading up to the movie. But the Dunkin' commercial makes it about as clear as possible.

That said, in the commercial, Gambit appears to be in Avengers tower, which means he's almost certainly on Earth 616 in the main MCU timeline. But when H.E.R.B.I.E. points to the planet in the sky, Gambit says "That's our world, there?" Channing Tatum has confirmed his Gambit is from the same world as the Fox-era X-Men. So when Gambit says, "That's our world there," is he talking about the Fox-era universe? Or H.E.R.B.I.E. and the Fantastic Four's universe? Likely the former, but it isn't entirely clear.

Channing Tatum was originally supposed to lead a Gambit movie during the 20th Century Fox X-Men era. But Tatum's Gambit movie failed after the studio tried to make it a comedy and once Disney bought the studio, it seemed that was the end of the whole thing. Tatum thought he'd lost Gambit forever before "Deadpool & Wolverine" came along and gave him a chance to finally debut as Remy Etienne LeBeau, the New Orleans native with the power to manipulate kinetic energy.

But Tatum's Gambit accent created a hilarious problem on "Deadpool & Wolverine." Because the movie was infused with the irreverent comedy of Ryan Reynolds' previous Deadpool outings, Gambit was made to use a heavy Cajun accent that essentially made him unintelligible — a joke that caused his co-stars to crack up on-set. But it seems he'll be toning down the accent for "Doomsday," as evidenced by the Dunkin' commercial.

The actor previously told Variety, "I'm not gonna go full Cajun. [Directors Anthony and Joe Russo] want things to be funny, but they don't want to go full 'Deadpool.' They want to keep the drama and keep it tight." This Dunkin' commercial is the first time we've been able to hear just how Tatum's new Gambit accent will sound, and it's certainly more intelligible than in "Deadpool & Wolverine."

Otherwise, "Avengers: Doomsday" is shaping up to be a tumultuous moment for the MCU, both on and off-screen, with Marvel Studios attempting to bring audiences back on board following a less than impressive Multiverse Saga. We'll have to see how it all pans out when the movie hits theaters on December 18, 2026.

Meet the history-makers of the 120th Congress

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

The incoming freshman class of the 120th Congress includes several people who scored historic wins in their states and the nation at large.

Among them were women breaking glass ceilings in the Senate and trailblazers for Native American representation. Beyond Washington, a handful of nominees pulled off milestone victories in gubernatorial elections, including four Black candidates. Congress is also poised to witness history next year with New York Democrat Hakeem Jeffries expected to become the first Black speaker.

Here are some of the candidates who made history on election night, both in Congress and in the governor’s mansions:

A member of the White Earth Band of Ojibwe, Flanagan becomes the first Native American woman elected to the Senate with her victory over Republican Michele Tafoya. The outgoing Minnesota lieutenant governor is also the first Native American to represent the state in Congress.

Andrews, a pediatrician, becomes the first woman elected to the Senate from South Carolina after defeating appointed Republican incumbent Darline Graham, the first female senator from the Palmetto State.

Letlow, the first Republican woman elected to the House from Louisiana, earns the same distinction in the Senate with her victory over Democrat Jamie Davis in the open-seat race to succeed GOP Sen. Bill Cassidy.

With his victory over Republican Mike Rogers, El-Sayed, the son of Egyptian immigrants, becomes the first Muslim person elected to the Senate.

By defeating former Sen. John E. Sununu, Pappas, a fourth-term congressman, becomes the first openly gay man elected to the Senate.

Kiros, who upset Rep. Diana DeGette in the Democratic primary for Colorado’s 1st District, easily won the general election to become the first Black woman elected to Congress from the Centennial State. Kiros, who was born in Addis Ababa, Ethiopia, and emigrated to the U.S. as an infant, is also the first Gen Z woman elected to Congress.

Koh, the son of a Korean American father and a Lebanese American mother, is the first Asian American elected to Congress from Massachusetts. The former Biden administration alumnus will succeed Democrat Seth Moulton in the state’s 6th District.

The daughter of immigrants from Chile and Cuba, Powell is the first Latino person elected to Congress from Nebraska after flipping the state’s 2nd District. She is also the first Democratic woman elected to Congress from Nebraska.

Dandiya, the daughter of Indian immigrants, becomes the first South Asian American elected to Congress from Florida with her victory in the redrawn 22nd District. She is also the first adherent of Jainism to be elected to Congress.

Ford, a state senator, unseated Republican Rep. Victoria Spartz to become the first openly gay person elected to Congress from Indiana.

McCann, a state senator, defeated Republican Rep. Bill Huizenga to become the first Native American elected to Congress from Michigan. McCann is a member of the Citizen Potawatomi Nation.

Moss, the president pro tempore of the Michigan Senate, keeps Rep. Haley Stevens’ 11th District in Democratic hands, becoming the first openly gay person elected to Congress from Michigan.

Hamawy, a former Army surgeon who emigrated from Egypt to the U.S. as an infant, is the first Muslim person elected to Congress from New Jersey, with his victory in the state’s 12th District.

With her victory in New York’s 13th District, Avila Chevalier becomes the first Muslim person elected to Congress from the Empire State. She succeeds Democratic Rep. Adriano Espaillat, whom she defeated in the primary.

A citizen of the Ysleta del Sur Pueblo Nation, Valdez is the first Native American elected to Congress from New York. The New York State assemblywoman will succeed retiring Democratic Rep. Nydia M. Velázquez.

Alfonso, the son-in-law of Transportation Secretary Sean Duffy, is the first Gen Z Republican elected to Congress with his victory in Wisconsin’s open 7th District, a seat his father-in-law once held.

Unofficial returns show Sarauw winning the open-seat race to succeed outgoing Del. Stacey Plaskett, making her the first openly gay person elected to Congress from the U.S. Virgin Islands.

The son of Mexican immigrants, Becerra, a former congressman and Health and Human Services secretary, is the first Latino to be elected governor of California. He is the state’s second Latino governor after Romualdo Pacheco, who was never elected to the role but briefly succeeded an incumbent in 1875.

Republican Rep. Byron Donalds will become Florida’s first Black governor after he defeated Democrat David Jolly, a former GOP congressman.

With her victory over Republican businessman Rick Jackson, Lance Bottoms becomes the first woman to be elected governor of Georgia. The former Atlanta mayor is also the state’s first Black governor and the first Black woman to be elected governor anywhere in the country, along with Del. Stacey Plaskett in the U.S. Virgin Islands (see entry below).

Klobuchar, the first woman to be elected senator from Minnesota, makes history again with her election as her state’s first female governor.

Ford, the current Nevada attorney general, will become his state’s first Black governor after defeating Republican incumbent Joe Lombardo.

Haaland, a member of the Pueblo of Laguna, will become the first Native American woman to be elected governor in the U.S. after defeating Republican Gregg Hull. The former Interior secretary was also one of the first Native American women elected to Congress, along with Kansas Rep. Sharice Davids, in 2018.

With her victory over Republican Vivek Ramaswamy, Acton becomes the first woman elected governor of Ohio. Republican Nancy Hollister briefly served as the state’s top executive in the late 1990s but was never elected governor.

Blackburn, Tennessee’s first female senator, will soon become the Volunteer State’s first female governor after her victory over Democrat Jerri Green.

Hinojosa, a state representative, will become the first Hispanic person elected Texas governor since statehood after she unseated three-term Republican incumbent Greg Abbott.

Crowley, the Milwaukee County executive, will be the first Black person elected governor of Wisconsin after he defeated Republican Rep. Tom Tiffany.

Unofficial returns show Plaskett, the nonvoting delegate from the U.S. Virgin Islands, becoming the first woman to be elected governor of the island territory. She is also one of the first Black women to be elected governor in the country, along with Georgia’s Keisha Lance Bottoms.

Amex Gold Card Review: Benefits & Points For Those Who Like To Eat

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

The American Express® Gold Card is one of the most rewarding cards out there if you’re looking to earn Amex Membership Rewards points.

The Amex Gold Card is an absolute powerhouse if you spend a lot on food, whether it’s at restaurants or supermarkets (or a combination of both). While the card has a somewhat steep annual fee, the good news is that it offers credits that can largely help offset it. We’re also currently seeing an exceptional welcome offer, making it the ideal time to pick up this card. Anyway, enough of an intro, let’s get into my in-depth Amex Gold Card review.

Link: Learn more about the American Express® Gold Card

Before I get into my full Amex Gold Card review, let me summarize some of the most important features:

Here are my thoughts on the major pros and cons of the Amex Gold Card:

For someone looking to earn Membership Rewards points, the Amex Gold Card is one of the most rewarding cards out there. To start, let’s take a look at the welcome offer on the card, along with the eligibility requirements and fees. After all, these are important factors people consider when applying for a card.

The Amex Gold Card has an improved welcome offer: You may be eligible for as high as 100,000 Membership Rewards® Points after spending $8,000 in eligible purchases on your new Card in your first 6 months of Membership. Welcome offers vary and you may not be eligible for an offer.

The way this works, you can apply for the card, and then find out your offer amount. Then you can decide whether you want to accept the offer or not. That’s an unconventional approach for a welcome offer. At least Amex has the “apply with confidence” feature, so there won’t be a hard credit pull unless you accept the offer.

Based on my latest miles & points valuations, Membership Rewards points are worth 1.7 cents each. So if you are eligible for a bonus of 100,000 points, those are worth $1,700, by my valuation.

This is a huge bonus, and on top of that, you have a full six months to complete the minimum spending requirement, which should be easy enough for many people to accomplish, as that translates to roughly $1,333 per month in required spending. Personally I’d only apply for the card if I got a welcome offer of at least 60,000 points (or ideally 80,000 points), but everyone has to decide for themselves what they’re willing to “settle” for.

The welcome bonus on the Amex Gold Card isn’t available to those who have or have had the card, or those who have had the Premier Rewards Gold Card in the past. Furthermore, those who have or have had an American Express Platinum Card® (learn more) may also not be eligible for the welcome offer on the card.

However, if you have had one of these cards before, you’re still eligible for the card, but you’re just not eligible for the welcome offer (which is “once in a lifetime”). In some circumstances, it could still be worth getting the card in those situations, if you’d benefit from it on an ongoing basis.

Let me emphasize that you’ll want to apply for the Amex Gold before you apply for the Amex Platinum. That’s because if you have the Amex Gold, you’re still eligible for the Amex Platinum offer, while the inverse isn’t true.

See this post for all the rules on applying for Amex cards, and see this post for details on getting approved for the Amex Gold Card.

The Amex Gold Card has a $325 annual fee. There is no annual fee for up to five additional cards. Meanwhile, the annual fee for six or more additional cards is $35 for each card (Rates & Fees).

Note that Amex Gold Card authorized users don’t receive some of the same benefits as the primary cardmember, but their spending does count toward the rewards that the primary cardmember earns.

The Amex Gold Card has no foreign transaction fees (Rates & Fees), so you can use it for purchases globally. That’s especially useful when you consider that you can earn 4x points on dining abroad, all with no foreign transaction fees.

What makes the Amex Gold Card so valuable is the bonus categories it offers, in the form of Membership Rewards points. The bonus categories include the following:

This is an outstanding return on spending. Based on my valuation of Amex points at 1.7 cents each, that’s like an 8.5% return on eligible prepaid hotel stays, a 6.8% return at restaurants and at U.S. supermarkets, and a 5.1% return on eligible flights.

Now let’s take a closer look at just how those bonus categories are defined.

The Amex Gold Card offers 5x Membership Rewards points on prepaid hotels that are booked through Amex Travel. As a general rule of thumb, this isn’t worth it. That’s because you’re usually not earning loyalty points with your preferred hotel program when you book through Amex Travel, so that’s quite an opportunity cost.

With the Amex Gold Card you’ll earn 4x points on the first $50,000 spent every calendar year at restaurants worldwide, and after that you’ll earn a single point per dollar spent.

Generally speaking, restaurants are defined pretty broadly, and the category includes everything from fast food to cafes to fine dining. This even includes delivery and takeout, given the popularity of that nowadays.

Just note that convenience stores, nightclubs, and restaurants located within other establishments won’t typically qualify for bonus points. Furthermore, eligibility for bonus points is determined based on how the merchant chooses to be categorized. There’s a chance that some merchants may not be categorized as a restaurant, even though you might consider them a restaurant.

For dining, this card even beats the 3x points that the Chase Sapphire Preferred® Card (learn more) and Chase Sapphire Reserve® (learn more) offer — this is one of the best rewards cards for dining purchases.

The Amex Gold Card offers 4x points on the first $25,000 spent every calendar year at U.S. supermarkets, and after that you’ll earn a single point per dollar. This excludes superstores and warehouse clubs, so don’t expect you’ll be able to earn those bonus points at Sam’s Club, for example. This is one of the best rewards cards for grocery store purchases.

The Amex Gold Card offers 3x points on airfare, with no limits on how many bonus points you can earn. This only applies when booking directly with an airline, through the Amex Travel App™ or through amextravel.com. Furthermore, charter flights and private jet flights are excluded.

Officially, booking through online travel agencies (except Amex Travel) wouldn’t earn you bonus points, though in practice, it may still sometimes code for 3x points. That’s because often online travel agencies still have the airline bill you directly when you book flights through them. This is one of the best rewards cards for airfare purchases.

The Amex Gold Card also offers 2x points on prepaid car rentals and cruises booked through Amex Travel. This bonus category doesn’t make or break the card, but it’s a nice extra, especially if you already make these bookings occasionally.

The Amex Gold Card earns Membership Rewards points. This is one of the major transferable points currencies, and is a favorite points currency for many.

The best way to redeem your Amex points is to transfer them to one of the Membership Rewards airline or hotel partners. Points can be transferred to about 20 partners, consisting primarily of airline loyalty programs, but also of some hotel loyalty programs.

The reason this represents such a good deal is that you can get outsized value toward first and business class travel. These tickets are often disproportionately expensive if paying cash, while they can be a good deal on points. On top of that, Amex often has transfer bonuses, which can stretch your points even further.

While you can redeem Membership Rewards points toward the cost of merchandise or gift cards, or to pay off your statement, these aren’t good uses of your points. You’ll typically get less than 1.0 cent of value per point, so that’s not how I’d redeem them.

In general, I’d recommend earning Membership Rewards points if your primary interest is transferring points to partner programs.

The Amex Gold Card offers a variety of benefits that can help offset the annual fee of the card. In particular, the Amex Gold offers statement credits for eligible purchases, which could save you up to $424 per year, more than offsetting the annual fee. Let’s take a closer look at how these work (Enrollment is required for select benefits).

The first perk is that the Amex Gold Card offers a dining credit of up to $120 per year. There are some restrictions to be aware of with this credit:

The Amex Gold Card offers up to $120 in Uber Cash annually:

This is a fantastic benefit and should be worth face value for anyone who uses Uber at least once a month. Even if you don’t use Uber, this can be useful for getting food delivery through Uber Eats.

The Amex Gold Card offers up to $100 in Resy credits annually:

The Amex Gold Card offers up to $84 in Dunkin’ credits annually:

One thing I’d mention is that anecdotally this also works for Dunkin’ reloads, so you can always get the Dunkin’ app, and then load $7 onto your account each month, and that should trigger the credit. That’s a great way to use this to “bank” credit with Dunkin’ toward a future purchase.

One of the great features of Amex cards is access to Amex Offers, which offers savings or bonus points on purchases with all kinds of retailers. There’s huge value to be had in getting as many Amex cards as possible, so that you can get these offers on multiple cards. Amex Offers could save you hundreds of dollars per year — just note that enrollment is required to take advantage of individual offers.

For staying two or more nights at select luxury hotels, you can receive extra benefits through The Hotel Collection. Benefits include a $100 hotel credit and a room upgrade, if available. This is different than Amex Fine Hotels + Resorts®, which requires having the Amex Platinum Card.

Holding the Amex Gold Card entitles you to a complimentary upgrade to Hertz Five Star status (enrollment required). This gets you an upgrade to a higher car class on rentals when available as well as a 25% bonus on all Hertz rentals. If you already rent cars through Hertz, this is a nice extra. It’s one of the more recently added benefits on the Amex Gold Card, too, since it was introduced in celebration of the card’s 60th anniversary.

The Amex Gold Card comes with a handful of travel insurance benefits that can protect your trip when you pay with your card. Check the card’s benefits guide for exact exclusions and limits, but here’s a quick overview:

The Amex Gold Card is a great card for anyone who spends a lot of money on food (whether at restaurants or supermarkets), and who is looking to earn Membership Rewards points. Furthermore, the card potentially offers over $400 in annual credits, between the dining credit, Resy credit, Dunkin’ credit, and Uber Cash.

I’d argue that if you value earning Membership Rewards points, if you spend a lot on dining, and if you can use a decent number of the card’s credits, then picking up this card is a slam dunk. It’s hard to beat this kind of return on dining and supermarket spending, and it’s a great complement to some other Amex cards.

The great welcome offer that’s available is the icing on the cake, and makes applying for the card all the more tempting. I’d recommend confirming you’re eligible for a welcome bonus on the higher end of the spectrum with the current offer of as high as 100,000 points, especially because you can only get an Amex Gold Card welcome bonus once.

I will say, the Amex Gold Card might not be for you if you don’t spend a lot on dining, if you don’t live in a major city where you can easily use the credits, if you value airport lounge access as a card perk, or if you’d rather earn cash back than travel rewards, since those are all potential considerations with the card.

It’s also worth comparing the Amex Gold Card to other options. Here’s how the Amex Gold Card stacks up against some of the most popular alternatives.

The Amex Platinum Card (learn more) has a higher annual fee than the Amex Gold and comes with many more perks and annual credits. Here are the biggest differences to know about:

If you travel many times a year and want as many benefits as possible, the Amex Platinum Card is hard to beat. That said, the Amex Gold Card still offers a better return on spending when it comes to dining out and shopping at U.S. supermarkets. As I see it, the cards serve different purposes — one is about the perks, and the other is about the return on spending.

If you’re looking for a rewards card with a lower annual fee, the Chase Sapphire Preferred Card (learn more) is worth comparing against the Amex Gold Card as well. It has a much lower annual fee of $95 and fewer perks, but it still earns very strong rewards:

Between the Amex Gold Card and the Chase Sapphire Preferred, the best option for you could come down to whether you prefer Amex Membership Rewards points or Chase Ultimate Rewards points. Make sure to compare your welcome bonus offers between both cards before making a final decision as well.

The Citi Strata Premier® Card (learn more) is another great rewards card with useful bonus categories at the $95 annual fee level:

If you want just one rewards card in your wallet, the Citi Strata Premier makes a strong case for itself with its bonus categories. I value both Amex points and Citi ThankYou points at 1.7 cents apiece, but some travelers might prefer one rewards program over the other depending on their favorite airline and hotel partners.

The Capital One Venture Rewards Credit Card (learn more) is one of my favorite cards for those looking for a simple strategy, and who don’t want to focus on a bunch of credits, maximizing bonus categories, etc.

The card has a $95 annual fee, and offers a minimum of 2x miles per dollar spent, making this one of the best cards for everyday spending. The reasonable annual fee plus the flat 2x miles is ideal for someone who doesn’t maybe spend a huge amount in categories that are otherwise eligible for bonus points.

The Amex Gold Card is one of American Express’ best rewards cards for earning points. With 4x points on dining and supermarkets, earning Membership Rewards points is easy.

The card also offers some credits that are potentially useful, which for many will help offset the annual fee. The card offers up to $120 dining credits, up to $120 in Uber Cash annually, up to $100 in Resy credits annually, and up to $84 in Dunkin’ credits annually — a total of $424 in annual credits if you take advantage of all of them. If you don’t yet have the card, it’s seriously worth considering.

Ripple Executive Reveals Massive Growth of Company's London Hub

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

According to Ripple's UK and Europe Managing Director, Cassie Craddock, Ripple’s London office has expanded significantly over the last decade to become Ripple’s largest international hub outside the U.S.

“When we moved into our current office in 2018, there were 2 floors, and a lot of empty desks. Now we’re on 3 floors and full on all the desks. We’re now over capacity,” she wrote on X.

Craddock's comments came after she took part in a fireside chat at the British Residence in New York as part of Ripple’s Swell conference.

She revealed that Ripple chose to focus on London early on, given the UK's strong capital markets, mature legal system, experienced regulators and concentration of financial talent.

However, she added that regulatory uncertainty had previously limited the company’s capacity to build more relationships with leading U.K. financial institutions.

Ripple's story in the U.K. capital began on March 21, 2016, when the San Francisco-based company officially announced the launch of its first office in London, located in Canary Wharf.

Back then, the expansion was meant to accommodate the growing interest of European banks in distributed ledger technology and cross-border settlement solutions.

Ripple incorporated its U.K. entity in April 2016 under the name Ripple Europe Ltd. (later changed to Ripple Markets UK Ltd. in August 2022).

Ripple continued to expand its ties to U.K. financial institutions in the following years.

In April 2018, Santander introduced its new international payments app, One Pay FX, which used Ripple’s xCurrent technology.

In February 2022, Ripple announced a partnership with London-based payments company Modulr to make it easier to send cross-border payments to the UK and Europe.

Moreover, Ripple has played a role in developing the research landscape for blockchain in the U.K. In January 2026, Ripple shared that it had pledged more than £5M to UK universities under its University Blockchain Research Initiative.

One of the major milestones for Ripple in the U.K. occurred on Jan. 9, when Ripple received an Electronic Money Institution license and cryptoasset registration from the Financial Conduct Authority. The authorizations enabled Ripple to extend its regulated cross-border payment services to UK institutions.

In February, Ripple also secured a partnership with Aviva Investors to explore the tokenization of traditional investment funds.

This work resulted in the launch of a tokenized share class of Aviva Investors’ £22bn Sterling Liquidity Fund on the XRP Ledger on Oct. 26.

Kim Kardashian’s Latest ‘Raspberry Cream’ Nails Are So Unexpected

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

The manicure is a sharp departure from her typical style.

Every item on this page was chosen by an ELLE editor. We may earn commission on some of the items you choose to buy.

Like the rest of her beauty looks, Kim Kardashian’s nails are often bold. She’s been known to sport long, almond- and coffin-shaped styles that grab your attention (she even added a bedazzled “P” to a Barbie pink manicure once in honor of her then-boyfriend Pete Davidson). But at the season 2 premiere of All’s Fair this week, Kardashian stepped out with short, square-shaped nails in an opaque light pink color. We’re dubbing them “raspberry cream” nails.

“It’s a beautiful interpretation of futuristic minimalism, featuring a soft, milky, almost aquatic pink with a delicate translucency,” says celebrity nail technician and creator of eNailCouture Max Estrada. “I believe the decision to keep the nails short was very intentional. The manicure wasn’t necessarily meant to be the focal point. Instead, it allowed the extraordinary jewelry and the other elements of the look to take center stage.”

And while square nails have taken a backseat to more rounded shapes like almond in recent years, Estrada says that when paired with feminine colors like raspberry cream, they can work beautifully. “Square nails have a certain architectural quality, but when the edges are gently softened, as they are here, they can communicate incredible delicacy and softness,” he says. “In this particular manicure, the square silhouette feels refined rather than severe.” Orly manicurist Riyah Martin agrees, calling short and square nails “cute and chic.”

To re-create the look, start with a short, square nail, and buff out the corners. “The idea is to retain that beautiful square architecture while removing any harshness from the edges,” says Estrada. “For the color, I would apply a thin layer of semi-sheer pink, followed by a delicate milky pink to create that ethereal, translucent neo-milky effect.” Add a glossy top coat, and to emulate Kardashian, be sure to finish with sparkly jewelry.

A Guide to the Blofelds

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

There’s a lot of talk going around lately about the casting of the new James Bond. Who will play him… Callum Turner, Jack Lowden, CrimeReads favorite/dark horse Josh Bowman? This question is all well and good… but I want to know if this new leg of the franchise is going to have a Blofeld. Because, notoriously, the 007 films can’t keep it together around this guy.

Who is Blofeld? Ernst Stavro Blofeld, Bond’s archnemesis. He’s a megalomaniac who keeps trying to take over the world in one way or another. Oftentimes, his face is hidden. Sometimes, he’s in a wheelchair. Usually, he is petting a cat. One time, he’s Bond’s adopted brother, kind of.

The whole Blofeld situation is a bit nuts, with a total of nine different actors playing him (in various capacities), across the different chapters of the franchise.

A preamble: EON became embroiled in a legal dispute surrounding Thunderball that had serious implications for the different Blofelds. As noted in the book The Battle for Bond by Robert Sellers, Ian Fleming had based his novel Thunderball on a 1959 script for a film that he had written with his associates Jack Whittingham, Kevin McClory and Ernest Cuneo. Apparently, the story changed considerably and the writers went through multiple versions. In March of 1961, McClory, read an advance copy of the novel and contacted Whittingham, with whom he petitioned the courts to slap the novel with an injunction to stop it from being published. The judge who heard the case that month, permitted the novel to continue being published, though he didn’t ban any further legal action from taking place. Fleming suffered a heart attack during this time, but survived.

The suit (McClory and Whittingham v Fleming, Jonathan Cape Ltd and Bryce) went to court in November of 1963, and the case lasted three weeks. Fleming had two more heat attacks during this process. The men settled the case out of court, agreeing that Fleming was to retain the rights to the novel (as long as further editions credited the story as having been created by McClory and Whittingham as well as Fleming), while McClory and Whittingham won film rights. Fleming died of a fourth heart attack the following year.

What does any of this have to do with Blofeld? Walk with me.

First, multiple actors played the role as a continuous character in the Connery era: Anthony Dawson as Blofeld’s body and Eric Pohlmann as his voice in both From Russia with Love (1963) and Thunderball (1965), then Donald Pleasance in You Only Live Twice (1967). This change-up of actors poses no real issue to the films, because we don’t see his face at all in those first two films; the first time we get to see his face, it’s Pleasance. Continuous enough, fine.

Here’s where it gets wacky. Well, wackier. After You Only Live Twice, EON (the company that produces the Bond films) cast a new 007, George Lazenby, to begin a new era of the franchise. Telly Savalas plays Blofeld in that film, On Her Majesty’s Secret Service (1969). But then Lazenby quit, setting the Bond franchise adrift. They brought back Connery to play Bond in 1971’s Diamonds are Forever, and so cast a new Blofeld: Charles Gray.

This was Connery’s last EON Bond film, with the producers bringing on Roger Moore to start a new era with 1973’s Live and Let Die. But the battle between Kevin McClory and the Fleming estate continued; the original 1963-64 settlement gave way to more lawsuits over who owned what. During this time, EON was not allowed to use any of the elements from Thunderball, which seemed to include the character Blofeld and the organization SPECTRE.

EON had a thing or two to say about how little the Bond franchise needed Blofeld. In a bizarre pre-credits sequence in the 1981 Moore film For Your Eyes Only, Bond casually kills Blofeld by using a helicopter to lift him up and drop him (robotic wheelchair, cat, and all) into a smoke stack, before proceeding with its main narrative. That Blofeld also didn’t show his face; his body was played by John Hollis with voicework by Robert Rietty. He’s also not referred to specifically as Blofeld (for legal reasons, obviously). But the message was clear.

Later legal verdicts in the battle for the rights to Thunderball, especially a landmark one in 1983, confirmed that McClory in fact owned numerous elements of the Thunderball story, and therefore the Bond franchise, as well as that he could make his own movie adaptation of Bond elements as long as they were based on the Thunderball film. So he made his own film, Never Say Never Again, that year, casting Max von Sydow as Blofeld, and Sean Connery as Bond.

Blofeld remains absent for the final Moore film A View to a Kill (1985), both Timothy Dalton films, all four Pierce Brosnan films, and the first three Daniel Craig films.

McClory, who continued to sue for rights and royalties to the Bond properties for the rest of his life, died in 2006. His family resolved the issues with the Fleming estate and EON productions, selling the rights to EON’s parent company in 2007, paving the way for Blofeld’s eventual return in Spectre (2015). In that film, and in the final Craig film No Time to Die (2021), he is played by Christoph Waltz, marking the first time that a single actor has played Blofeld (in both body and voice) across two films.

Olivia Rutigliano is an Editor at Lit Hub and CrimeReads. Her other work appears in Vanity Fair, Vulture, Lapham's Quarterly, AirMail, the Los Angeles Review of Books, Public Books, The Baffler, Bright Wall/Dark Room, Politics/Letters, The Toast, Truly Adventurous, and elsewhere. She has a PhD from the departments of English/comparative literature and theatre at Columbia University, where she was the Marion E. Ponsford fellow. She hosts the podcasst "Culture Schlock" at Lit Hub Radio. She is on instagram at @oldebean, twitter at @oldrutigliano, and bluesky at @oliviarutigliano.bsky.social.

God of War: Laufey has Been Delayed for Additional Polishing

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

Michael Manning (He/Him) has been enamored with video games since he first played the Sonic Classic Collection as a child. With decades of experience in gaming and a full tour as a Gaming Journalist, Michael is ready to step up to the plate. From AAA to the smallest passion projects, his motto is that every piece of ar

February 2027 is set to be a massive release window, with remakes and new releases of all kinds of major series hitting one after another. Because of this, a few titles have pushed their release dates back, opting instead to give February releases their time and allowing gamers' wallets a bit of time to recover.

Wuthering Waves is one of the biggest Gachas on the planet, and they do far better than most at answering player complaints. Let's go over exactly how!

Santa Monica Studio joins the teams leaning away from February 2027, as the previously planned God of War: Laufey has been delayed to May 20th, 2027. With how important Laufey’s individual performance needs to be, Santa Monica Studio is making the most of the time they have, refusing to fall for the age-old pull of rushing release in order to present the best game possible.

Santa Monica Studio is easily one of the most prestigious teams under Sony, releasing banger after banger with the God of War series. Coming off the announcement of remakes of the original trilogy, Santa Monica revealed a major gamble with God of War Laufey, the first title in the franchise without Kratos as the central playable character.

The game focuses on Kratos’ wife and Atreus’s mother, Laufey, one of the last Norse giants present in the new God of War’s setting. Trapped within a mystical realm of the afterlife, Laufey seems to be taking major notes from the PS2/PS3 God of War titles, pitting its protagonist against a truly magical set of foes with only one way out.

After some of the more grounded presentation and general combat of the last two God of War titles, Laufey seems like a perfect return to form, one that the team is making sure hits as hard as possible. The release date change is disappointing for eager fans, but it does wonders to dodge some other heavy hitters dropping in February.

If Laufey stuck to its original release plan, the game would be battling titans like Fable, Persona 4 Revival, and Metro 2039, all major releases gamers are setting aside cash and time for. Not that God of War Laufey wouldn’t be able to hang, but it's worth taking a step back if the team could afford to.

Being one of Sony’s flagship studios doesn’t mean that Santa Monica is free of danger, if their recent treatment of Suckerpunch is any indication, but ensuring a good release is best both for the team and the consumers. Laufey doesn’t have the immediate pull for long-time players who haven’t kept up with the new God of War titles, so it needs to stand on its own as strongly as possible.

Santa Monica is a team known for absolute polish, and their refusal to be rushed is a great sign that maintaining this quality is a top priority. Too many games have been released in recent years only to be cannibalized by post-launch patches that add functions that should already be there, a mistake Santa Monica Studio is being careful to avoid.

After some of the more grounded presentation and general combat of the last two God of War titles, Laufey seems like a perfect return to form, one that the team is making sure hits as hard as possible.

May is also a much cushier release month in terms of competing releases, with no major drops ready to clash with God of War Laufey’s acclaim. The new title by Santa Monica Studio may feel like a spinoff meant to buy time, but the opportunity to learn more about Laufey, such an impactful character in the new God of War series, is incredibly exciting for any fans of that saga.

Finally getting her side of the story is a phenomenal way of closing the saga for now, allowing Kratos to rest on his laurels while expanding the wildly fantastical side of not only the Norse saga, but the entire worldwide mythos of God of War.

Hyrule has never looked better as they look in The Legend of Zelda: Ocarina of Time Remake, and critics seem to agree!
